## 19. ATT+bal-2L: ATT+bal as level 1 of the two-level Nebula (plan written 2026-11-20, BEFORE any code for it was run and before any training)

**Question.** ATT+bal (§14) reads only the first 512 tokens of a report. The length study (hier_nebula) showed that 99.8% of reports fit into 8 x 512 tokens. Does a model that reads up to 8 chunks, with ATT+bal as the chunk encoder, (a) keep the robustness that balanced padding gave ATT+bal and (b) catch what padding pushes out of the first 512 tokens (k = 100, 200 calls)? Model B (same two-level model, no padding) separates "reading more" from "padding". Branch: the code and this file are committed on the session branch `ccr-1ac1816e-y66901` (same commit as `nebula-baseline` at the start); nothing is pushed unless asked.

**19.1 Model "ATT+bal-2L".**
- *Level 1* (shared weights for every chunk) = the ATT+bal encoder: `baseline/pe.py` `TransformerEncoderChunksPE(pe_mode="paper", pool_mode="attn")`, BPE-50k (vocab 50,001), d 64, 8 heads, FFN 256, 2 layers, chunk-attention 64, dropout 0.3, norm_first. It is applied to each chunk of 512 tokens; **positions are 0..511 inside every chunk** (each chunk is a separate input of length 512, so the paper's sinusoidal PE restarts at 0 in every chunk; there is no global position across chunks, the order of the chunks is given to level 2 only). **The chunk vector is the 64-d attention-pooled vector** (`core()` output, i.e. the input of ATT+bal's classifier head). ATT+bal's own classifier head (64 -> 64 -> 1) is not part of the model (removed, so that it has no unused weights; its 4,225 parameters are not counted).
- *Level 2* = as `hier_nebula/model.py`: a learned [CLS] vector + the chunk vectors (no projection: 64 = 64) + a learned chunk-position embedding (9 positions: CLS, chunks 1 to 8), 2 pre-norm Transformer encoder layers (d 64, 4 heads, FFN 128, dropout 0.1), padded chunks masked (`src_key_padding_mask`), LayerNorm(CLS) -> Linear(64, 1) -> logit. Loss = BCE with logits.
- *Chunks.* The first 8 x 512 = 4,096 tokens of the whole report (no cut at 512); chunk c = tokens 512c .. 512c+511; the number of real chunks of a report = max(1, ceil(min(total tokens, 4096) / 512)); the last real chunk is filled with id 0 (= padding) up to 512 tokens; **only real chunks are run through level 1** (padding chunks are not computed, and are masked in level 2). The attention pooling ignores id-0 tokens, as in ATT+bal.
- *Parameters* (counted by the unit test and written here after the pilot; hand count before): level 1 about 3.30 M (embedding 50,001 x 64 = 3,200,064; two layers 2 x 49,856; query 64; plus whatever the pinned class adds), level 2 about 68 k (CLS 64, position embedding 576, two layers 2 x 33,472, final LayerNorm 128, linear 65), **total about 3.37 M** (ATT+bal: 3.30 M + head 4,225). Exact numbers: see 19.8.
- *Initial weights.* Level 1 is built right after `set_seed(seed)` exactly as for ATT+bal, so level 1 of seed k starts from the same weights as ATT+bal seed k (the head is then dropped); level 2 is initialised next in the random stream.

**19.2 Data.** The same 90% January slice (67,768 reports) and validation slice (7,530: 2,467 benign, 5,063 malware): `gap.val_slice` with the stored hash `b18269395891ee1e51d69e4a7293f0af` (= `baseline/gap_split_hash.json`, asserted in the driver and in the unit test; the existing assertion already compares the full 32-digit string, and the driver asserts the constant again). April test (17,402). Tokens come from the call-token store (`results/callstore` = `all.npz`, our BPE-50k; checked in §15: concatenation = full re-tokenisation) with the cut changed from 512 to 4,096. Nothing edited is written anywhere.

**19.3 Balanced padding (model A).** Exactly the plan of ATT+bal (`make_pad.plan_train_row`, stream 7000): per report of the training slice, malware and benign alike, with probability p = 0.5 an edit with k ~ U{1..50} added calls, insert (k random gaps) or prepend, 50/50; each added call = a uniform TRAIN-pool report (benign reports of the 90% slice with at least one call; validation and April excluded), then a uniform call of it; numpy `default_rng([7000, seed, epoch, row])`, so **new edits for every seed and epoch, and the edit plan of (seed, epoch, row) is identical to the one of ATT+bal**. The edit is applied to the WHOLE call list, the edited report is tokenised by concatenation, and only then is it cut into the first 4,096 tokens and chunked. (Test in the pilot: the first 512 tokens of every edited report equal the ATT+bal row of the same seed, epoch and row.) The padded report replaces the original in that epoch ("replace" mode as ATT+bal). Edits are made in memory per epoch. Validation is clean (unedited) in every run. Model B uses the same code with p = 0.

**19.4 Models and what is compared (10 seeds, 0 to 9, each).**
| model | level 1 init | training data |
|---|---|---|
| **A** ATT+bal-2L | scratch (or warm, see below) | balanced padding |
| **B** control | scratch | no padding |
Candidate for A only: **warm start** of level 1 from the trained ATT+bal weights of the same seed (`attbal/seed<k>.pt` of the private dataset `manishsinghxdev/nebula-review-models`: ATT+bal, lr 2e-3, p 0.5, epoch 2 = §14; the head keys are dropped, every other key must match, asserted; level 2 from scratch; the same lr for both levels; no frozen warm-up). It has seen 2 more epochs than a scratch model; this is stated next to the result. Warm vs scratch is decided on validation by the rule of 19.5, jointly with lr and epoch. B is not warm-started (no plain-data ATT model at the same pooling is used as a warm start; B is the clean control of the two-level architecture).

**19.5 Recipe and selection (validation only, EVAL_PROTOCOL.md 4).** AdamW, weight decay 1e-2, gradient clipping 0.5 (global norm over level 1 + level 2), dropout 0.3 in level 1 and 0.1 in level 2, constant learning rate (no scheduler, so the state after epoch e of a 3-epoch run is the state of an e-epoch run), effective batch 96 (706 optimizer steps per epoch), 3 epochs, one `epoch` = one pass over the (edited) training slice in a fresh random order (`default_rng([9100, seed, epoch])`). **Batch used:** as many reports per forward pass as fit on the T4 (the largest possible micro-batch is 96 reports = up to 768 chunks); if a micro-batch does not fit, the driver halves it and accumulates gradients to the same effective batch of 96 (loss = mean over the 96; the value used is logged). The pilot states which one was used. Precision fp32 (as every earlier run).
Grid (fixed now; no cell added after results are seen): learning rate in {1e-3, 2e-3} x epoch in {1, 2, 3}, each cell with all 10 seeds. A: init in {scratch, warm} -> 4 trained runs (2 lr x 2 init) x 3 epochs = 12 candidates (init, lr, epoch). B: 2 trained runs x 3 epochs = 6 candidates. Selection per model, rule of 16.4 exactly as the task states it: collapse guard (any seed with validation AUC < 0.6 at that epoch excludes the candidate); mean over 10 seeds of validation pAUC@1e-2; `best` = highest mean, SE = std (ddof 1) / sqrt(10); tied = within one SE of `best`; among tied: **fewest epochs, then lower lr**; if still tied for A: scratch before warm (my addition: fewer moving parts; written here before any result). (16.4 rule 2, the robustness filter R, is not used here: the question of this section is tested by the attacks in April, not on validation. 16.4's validation-loss tie-break is replaced by "lower lr" as the task says.) The number of tuned hyper-parameters differs between A (3: lr, epoch, init) and B (2: lr, epoch) and from ATT+bal / v2-PE (none tuned beyond their recipes); this is stated in the report. Selection is done by a script that opens validation numbers only; its output is committed before any April logit is opened.
**Logging.** After every epoch: validation metrics (pAUC@1e-2, AUC, TPR@1e-2, TPR@1e-3, ...), clean validation loss, mean training loss of the epoch and the mean BCE of every 10 training batches, and the clean loss on a fixed subset of 2,000 training reports (unedited tokens, eval mode), in the format of `results/loss_curves/<model>/seed<k>.json`. Validation logits, April logits and weights after every epoch are stored (April logits are stored, **not opened** until the choice is committed).

**19.6 Evaluation (April, once per final model: A chosen, B chosen).**
- Clean: pAUC@1e-2 (primary), TPR@1e-2, TPR@1e-3, AUC, F1 at logit 0, pAUC@1e-3; per-seed mean +- std and the 10-seed ensemble (mean of the logits). Claim rule of EVAL_PROTOCOL 5 (paired stratified bootstrap, 1,000 resamples, numpy seed 0, the same resamples; CI of the per-seed mean excludes 0 AND the same sign in >= 7 of 10 seeds) for A against ATT+bal (§14, the 10 existing models), against v2-PE and against B; the same **cluster bootstrap over `hash_apis`** as `baseline/audit_cluster.py`. Ensemble reported next to it.
- Per family TPR@1% FPR as the family slide (`results/hier/summary.md` layout: threshold at 1% FPR of the model's own benign April scores) and the **coinminer cluster** (`hash_apis` d91b96b4...) separately (`baseline/coinminer.py`).
- Attacks (edits built in memory from the call store, cut 4,096 for A and B and 512 for the one-chunk models; thresholds per model and seed at 1% and 0.1% FPR of the clean April benign scores; flagged = score above the threshold; scores saved, never tokens):
  1. random padding with EVAL-pool calls (benign reports of the validation slice), all April reports, k in {5, 20, 50, 100, 200} x {prepend, insert} (10 edit sets; `make_pad.plan_test_row` stream 6000, so k = 5, 20, 50 are the same edits as §14);
  2. surrogate-chosen padding: the existing LightGBM and v2-PE-seed-0 API-name rankings (`results/attack/ranking.json`, 1,031 names, not recomputed), the representative calls of ranks 1..k, k in {20, 50, 100}, insert and prepend, added to every April report (insert gaps `default_rng([8000, row, k, 0])`);
  3. white-box greedy: 500 random April malware (numpy seed 31), prepend, up to k = 50 steps, 200 random EVAL-pool candidates per step (numpy seed 41 + step, the same for every model and report), the candidate that lowers the model's own score most is prepended; a report at or below the 1% threshold stops; seeds 0 to 2 (more only if time allows). **Padded-benign FPR for every attack:** for 1 and 2 the benign April reports are edited by the same edits and scored with the same thresholds; for 3, the call list chosen for each of the 500 malware reports is prepended to one random April benign report (numpy seed 32, the same pairing for every model) and scored.
  Comparison on the same edits: ATT+bal and v2-PE (and B) re-scored on every edit set including k = 100 and 200 (their weights: `nebula-review-models`, `nebula-baseline-models`), seeds 0 to 9 for 1 and 2, seeds 0 to 2 for 3.
- Outputs: `results/attbal_2l/` (README in plain English, tables, loss curves, the figure detection vs k for k = 0, 5, ..., 200), a new section of `REPRODUCTION_REPORT.md`. No April number is used for any choice (candidates, epoch, init, edit parameters are all fixed before April is opened; the April appendix lists all candidates marked "not used for selection").

**19.7 Stages and GPU estimates (T4; estimates before measurement, to be replaced by the pilot).**
Inputs for the estimate: ATT+bal takes about 70 s of training per epoch per seed on one T4 (§16.7: two seeds in parallel on the two T4) plus 12 s for the edits; the mean number of 512-token chunks of a report is about 2.7 (REPORT 2: mean 2.8 uncapped, 99.8% fit in 8), so level 1 does about 3 times the work per epoch; padded training reports are slightly longer. Hand estimate: **about 4 to 5 min per seed-epoch** (3.5 to 3.9 min training, about 25 s of edits with the cut at 4,096, about 10 s of evaluation of validation and April).
- Stage 1 pilot (1 seed, 1 epoch, A scratch and B, lr 2e-3, plus unit tests): about 0.15 GPU-h.
- Stage 2 training: 6 trained runs (A scratch x 2 lr, A warm x 2 lr, B x 2 lr) x 10 seeds x 3 epochs = 180 seed-epochs x 4.5 min = **about 13.5 GPU-h** (about 7 h of wall time on two T4). **This is more than the earlier tuning budgets (§16: about 2.2 h) and may be more than the quota left; the pilot decides, and I stop and ask before Stage 2.** Options to cut it, none taken without approval: warm start for lr 2e-3 only; B with the lr and epoch chosen for A (B's own grid is 4.5 h of the 13.5); 2 epochs as the maximum.
- Stage 3: clean scoring is free (the April logits are stored). Random padding: 11 sets x 17,402 reports x 10 seeds with about 4 chunks per padded report (k = 200 prepend adds about 2,000 tokens, i.e. up to 8 chunks) = about 0.3 to 0.45 h per model; surrogate (13 sets) about 0.4 h per model; white-box: 500 reports x 50 steps x 200 candidates = 5 M padded reports per seed (every chunk boundary shifts when a call is prepended, so level 1 cannot be re-used) = **about 1 h per model and seed in the worst case, 3 h per model for seeds 0 to 2; this is the dominant cost**, to be measured in the pilot from the scoring throughput, with the early stop of evaded reports reducing it. Total for A, B and re-scoring ATT+bal and v2-PE at k = 100, 200: **about 8 to 10 h** (pre-pilot).
- Every stage is estimated again from measured numbers before it is run, and the quota left is reported (quota used by the other sections: §14 to §16; the second Kaggle account is used for this section, token from the environment, never printed or written to a file).

**19.8 Stage 1 pilot: what is checked and reported.** One seed (0), one epoch, A (scratch) and B, lr 2e-3, micro-batch 96 first. (1) Unit tests (CPU, run at the start of the kernel; the nebula package is cloned by the kernel as in every earlier kernel): (a) with K = 1 the two-level model reduces to a single chunk: for a report of <= 512 tokens the chunk is the report's first 512-token row, level 1 gives `core()` of ATT+bal for the same weights (bit-identical, tested with the stored ATT+bal seed-0 weights loaded as warm start), level 2 receives one real chunk and the 7 padding chunks change nothing; (b) a report of 1,000 tokens: chunk 2 is level 1 applied on its own to tokens 512..999 (padded), the position encoding of every chunk starts at 0, the logit does not depend on the other reports in the batch or on the content of the masked chunks; (c) the chunking of an edited 4,096+ token report keeps exactly 4,096 tokens; (d) the first 512 tokens of 200 edited reports equal the ATT+bal edits (`inmem_pad`) of the same (seed, epoch, row); (e) the validation hash; (f) the parameter counts. (2) Measured: seconds per epoch (training, edits, evaluation), peak GPU memory, micro-batch used, mean number of chunks per report in training and in April, scoring throughput (reports/s) clean and with k = 100, 200 prepend. (3) Validation numbers of the pilot epoch are looked at (no April). Then **stop and report the estimate; Stages 2 and 3 only after approval.**

**19.9 Rules for this section.** The Kaggle token is never printed, pasted or committed; datasets and kernels stay private; edited reports are built in memory and only scores are saved; no April result is used for any choice.

*Correction (2026-11-20, before any training result): in 19.2 the first version of this plan said that only 16 of the 32 digits of the validation hash are compared. That was wrong: `gap.val_slice` compares all 32 digits (the stored sha256 is already truncated to 32 hex digits); sentence corrected, nothing else changed.*

**19.10 Stage 1 pilot: result (2026-11-20; kernel `manishsinghxdev/nebula-2l-pilot`, second account, T4 x2, one GPU for A and one for B, wall 3.7 min = about 0.06 h of session time; files in `results/attbal_2l/pilot/`; no April logit was produced or looked at).**
Self tests (all passed on the real model, CPU): (a) K = 1: the chunk vector of 64 reports of <= 512 tokens equals `core()` of the stored ATT+bal seed-0 model **bit for bit** (max difference 0.0); changing the 7 padding chunks changes no logit (0.0). (b) 32 reports of 900-1000 tokens: chunk 2 equals level 1 applied to the same tokens as the first chunk of a report of its own (0.0, so positions restart at 0 in every chunk); logit alone vs inside a batch: max difference 1.9e-7; logit when the content beyond the real chunks changes: 0.0. (c) 34,019 edited reports (seed 0, epoch 1; = the 34,019 of ATT+bal), 90 of them with >= 4,096 tokens, all fill 8 chunks. (d) 201 edited reports: the first 512 tokens and the total token count equal the ATT+bal edit (`inmem_pad`) of the same (seed, epoch, row) (0 differences), and `build_fast` equals `build_tokens` at cut 4,096 (0 differences). (e) validation hash asserted; the first 512 tokens of all 92,700 call-store rows equal the stored BPE-50k rows. (f) **Parameters: level 1 3,300,096, level 2 67,777, total 3,367,873** (ATT+bal 3,304,321 = level 1 + its removed head 4,225).
Measured (one seed, one epoch, lr 2e-3, micro-batch 96 = the whole effective batch of 96 fitted; no gradient accumulation needed):
| | A (balanced padding) | B (no padding) |
|---|---|---|
| edit generation | 11.3 s | 0 |
| training, 706 steps | 105.1 s | 103.8 s |
| validation + 2,000-report train subset | 3.8 s | 3.8 s |
| epoch total (without April scoring and saving) | 120 s | 108 s |
| peak GPU memory allocated / reserved | 1.71 / 2.65 GB | 1.70 / 2.61 GB |
| mean real chunks per report: training epoch / validation | 2.86 / 2.74 | 2.76 / 2.74 |
Scoring throughput of the trained pilot model (1,000 validation malware reports, TRAIN-pool calls prepended): k = 0: 1,480 reports/s (A), 1,387 (B), mean 4.5 chunks (malware reports are longer than average); k = 100: 1,349 / 1,312 (5.6 chunks); k = 200: 1,118 / 1,081 (6.8 chunks); about 7,400 chunks/s per T4.
**Pilot validation numbers (seed 0, epoch 1, lr 2e-3; the grid is not decided by one seed): A pAUC@1e-2 0.306, TPR@1e-2 0.966, AUC 0.9912, val loss 0.0854; B pAUC@1e-2 0.953, TPR@1e-2 0.985, AUC 0.9984, val loss 0.0452** (ATT+bal at this lr and epoch 1: 0.950 mean over seeds, REPORT 5.12). The low pAUC of A is not a general failure: the model ranks well at FPR 1% (TPR 0.966) but about 21 of the 2,467 validation benign reports receive logits of 6.8 to 7.3, as high as the most confident malware (B: 2 such reports), so the first 1% of the benign ranking sits above most malware. One seed and one epoch cannot say whether this is a start-up effect that disappears in epochs 2 and 3 (the plan includes them) or a systematic effect of A. It is not acted on; it is reported here so that the epoch-1 to epoch-3 validation numbers of Stage 2 are read with it in mind.
**Measured estimate replaces 19.7.** Per seed-epoch with April scoring and weights saved: A about 130 s, B about 118 s (April scoring about 10 s). One run of 10 seeds x 3 epochs: about 1.1 GPU-h (about 0.55 h of wall time on two T4). Stage 2 (6 runs x 10 seeds x 3 epochs): **about 6.5 GPU-h = about 3.3 h of wall time** (plus about 3 min set-up per kernel), not 13.5 GPU-h. Stage 3 (scoring throughput above, white-box worst case without early stop): random padding about 0.45 GPU-h per model, surrogate about 0.55 per model, white-box 500 x 50 x 200 = 5 M padded reports per seed = about 1.1 GPU-h per seed and model (A and B, seeds 0-2: about 6.6 GPU-h; ATT+bal and v2-PE one chunk, about 0.3 GPU-h per seed: about 1.7); re-scoring the one-chunk models at k = 100, 200 about 0.3. **Stage 3 about 10 to 11 GPU-h (about 5 h of wall time), of which the white-box attack is 8 GPU-h**; early stopping of evaded reports lowers this, but a robust model gets no early stops. Whole programme about 17 GPU-h = about 8.5 h of wall time. Options to cut Stage 3 (not taken without approval): white-box for B on seed 0 only (saves 2.2 GPU-h); 100 instead of 200 candidates is NOT proposed because it changes the attack of §15.

**19.11 Stage 2 launched (2026-11-20, approved by the owner with "Don't worry about gpu and run"; the grid of 19.5 is unchanged, no cell added or dropped).** Two kernels in parallel on the second account, each a T4 x2 session with one seed per GPU: `nebula-2l-s2a` = A scratch lr 1e-3, A scratch lr 2e-3, B lr 1e-3; `nebula-2l-s2b` = A warm lr 1e-3, A warm lr 2e-3, B lr 2e-3; each cell 10 seeds (0-9) x 3 epochs; per seed and epoch the validation logits, April logits and weights are stored (April logits are not opened). Expected about 1.7 h per kernel. Selection (`baseline/twolevel_select.py`, validation numbers only) is written and committed before any April logit is opened. The pilot's A anomaly (21 high-scoring benign reports at epoch 1) is read from the epoch 1-3 validation numbers of all seeds; nothing is changed in response to it before the selection.

**19.12 Stage 2 finished and the setting chosen (2026-11-20; kernels `nebula-2l-s2a` and `nebula-2l-s2b`, both COMPLETE; written and committed BEFORE any April logit was opened. Only `seed*.json`, validation logits and logs were downloaded; the April logits and weights stay in the kernel output).** The grid of 19.5 was run as written: 6 cells x 10 seeds x 3 epochs, micro-batch 96 = effective batch 96 (no accumulation) in every unit, about 6.2 GPU-h of epochs (estimate in 19.10: 6.5). Mean seconds per epoch: A 129 s, B 116 s. Files: `results/attbal_2l/stage2/<cell>/seed<k>.json` (loss_curves format) and `..._val_logits.npy`, logs in `results/attbal_2l/stage2_logs/`; selection by `python -m baseline.twolevel_select` -> `selection.json`, `validation_table.md`.
Rule of 19.5 applied unchanged: no candidate collapsed (lowest validation AUC of any seed and epoch 0.9906); `best` = A warm lr 1e-3 epoch 1 (0.9438, SE 0.0051) and B lr 1e-3 epoch 3 (0.9631, SE 0.0051); tied cells are those within 1 SE of `best`; then fewest epochs, lower lr, scratch before warm.
| | cells tied with `best` | **chosen** |
|---|---|---|
| A (ATT+bal-2L) | A warm lr 1e-3 epoch 1 (0.9438), A scratch lr 1e-3 epoch 3 (0.9434) | **A: warm start of level 1, lr 1e-3, 1 epoch** (mean val pAUC@1e-2 0.9438, SE 0.0051) |
| B (control, no padding) | B lr 1e-3 epoch 3 (0.9631), B lr 2e-3 epoch 1 (0.9585) | **B: lr 2e-3, 1 epoch** (0.9585, SE 0.0032) |
Things to read with the choice (descriptive; nothing was changed because of them):
1. The two tied A cells differ by 0.0004. The rule picks warm / 1 epoch because it has fewer epochs. Level 1 of that candidate already had 2 epochs of ATT+bal training (the warm start), so "1 epoch" means 1 epoch of the two-level model on top of it; the total number of level-1 epochs is 3 for warm and 3 for scratch.
2. The pilot anomaly (seed 0, scratch lr 2e-3, epoch 1: pAUC 0.306) is **a single-seed spike, not a general failure of A**: scratch epoch-1 means are lowered by occasional bad seed-epochs (scratch lr 1e-3 epoch 1: seed 2 0.383, seed 4 0.640; scratch lr 2e-3 epoch 2: seed 5 0.650; warm lr 1e-3 epoch 2: seed 2 0.596; warm lr 2e-3 epoch 2: seed 0 0.499, seed 5 0.301). B has them too (B lr 1e-3 epoch 1 seed 4: 0.666). Such spikes are a few benign reports with very high logits. They make epoch-to-epoch curves jagged for every cell; the chosen cells have none (lowest seed: A 0.911, B 0.943).
3. Validation loss and pAUC do not always move together (A warm lr 1e-3: loss 0.0568 at epoch 1, 0.0628 at epoch 2). The selection metric is pAUC as pinned in 19.5; the loss is a tie-break that was not needed.
4. A had 4 candidate runs (2 lr x 2 init) and B 2, so A had more chances to be picked by chance; this is stated in the report as a limit (EVAL_PROTOCOL 4.3: free hyper-parameters differ between models). The chosen A and B both stop at epoch 1, so the April comparison A vs B is at equal training length of the two-level model (but A's level 1 carries 2 earlier epochs).
Stage 3 will evaluate **A (A_warm_lr1e-3, epoch 1)** and **B (B_lr2e-3, epoch 1)** on April once. The April logits of every other cell go into an appendix "not used for selection" (EVAL_PROTOCOL 4.5), written after the main April table.

**19.13 Stage 3 started (2026-11-20): clean April evaluation done; attacks launched.** The April logits of the chosen cells (A_warm_lr1e-3 epoch 1, B_lr2e-3 epoch 1) were opened only after 19.12 was committed. Clean results: `results/attbal_2l/clean/` (`baseline/twolevel_eval.py`; ATT+bal and v2-PE are the existing 10-seed models; the nebula `get_tpr_at_fpr` is replaced locally by its documented rule "last ROC point with FPR <= target", which reproduces the stored ATT+bal numbers to 5e-7). The comparison with the other cells of the grid (appendix "not used for selection") is written after the attack results.
Attack code: `baseline/twolevel_attack.py` (+ `baseline/tests/test_twolevel_attack.py`: the new greedy equals the original `attack_run.run_3b` step for step with a stand-in scorer; the 4,096-token build starts with the 512-token build). Weights of A and B (chosen cells only, renamed `a2l/seed<k>.pt`, `b2l/seed<k>.pt`), `ranking.json` and reference logits (A, B April logits of the training run; `a3pad_attbal.npz`, `a3pad_v2_pe.npz`) are in the private dataset `manishsinghxdev/nebula-2l-models`. Each scoring process first checks that its unedited scores equal the earlier April logits (tolerance 2e-3) and stops otherwise; ATT+bal and v2-PE must also reproduce their stored random-padding scores for k = 5, 20, 50.
**Estimate before running (measured scoring speed 1.1-1.5 k reports/s on a T4 for A and B; one-chunk models about 8x faster):** (1) scores of all edit sets: random 10 sets + surrogate 12 sets + unedited = 23 sets x 17,402 reports x 10 seeds; A and B about 1.1 GPU-h each, ATT+bal and v2-PE about 0.15 each: **about 2.5 GPU-h**. (2) White-box (500 reports x 50 steps x 200 candidates = 5 M padded reports per seed and model at the worst case, no early stop): A and B about 1.1-1.5 GPU-h per seed; ATT+bal and v2-PE (new 50-step run so that all four models have the same attack; steps 1-20 are the attack of REPORT 5.11) about 0.3 GPU-h per seed; seeds 0-2: **about 12 GPU-h**. Total about 14.5 GPU-h = about 4 h of wall time on three T4-x2 sessions. White-box models: A and B seeds 0-2 as asked; ATT+bal and v2-PE seeds 0-2.

## 20. ATT+bal-2L, round 2: can a better recipe reach the clean accuracy of the baselines? (plan written 2026-11-20, BEFORE any round-2 training; requested by the owner after the clean result of 19.13)

**Why.** Clean April (19.13): A 0.687 pAUC@1e-2, ATT+bal 0.707, v2-PE 0.705 (A is 2.0 points below ATT+bal, resolved by the stratified bootstrap with 8/10 seeds, not by the cluster bootstrap; not resolved against v2-PE). The 10-seed ensemble of A (0.736) is much better than its single models (0.687), so a large part of the gap looks like training noise. In Stage 2 several seeds had one bad epoch (a few benign reports with very high scores) in every cell, with a constant learning rate and only {1e-3, 2e-3} tried. These are **hypotheses**, not findings: the changes below may change nothing, and a gain of about 2 points is smaller than the seed spread (1.7 to 3 points).
**Changes tested (each in the cell named; everything else as in 19.5: AdamW, wd 1e-2, clip 0.5, dropout 0.3 / 0.1, effective batch 96, 706 steps per epoch, balanced padding p 0.5 on the whole call list, 4,096 tokens):**
1. *Learning-rate decay:* the lr falls linearly from its peak to 0 over the 3 epochs of the run (step-wise). Because the state after epoch e now depends on the 3-epoch schedule, a candidate "epoch 1 or 2" is a checkpoint inside the 3-epoch schedule (lr still high), not a short run.
2. *Lower learning rate:* peak 5e-4 with decay, and a constant 3e-4.
3. *Weight averaging (EMA):* an exponential moving average of all weights, decay 0.999 per step with the usual warm-up min(0.999, (1+n)/(10+n)); evaluated at every epoch end next to the raw weights. It costs no extra training, so **every cell reports both** and "raw / EMA" is a candidate dimension.
4. *Level-1 learning rate x 0.25* (level 2 at the full rate) and 5. *Level 1 frozen for the first epoch* (706 steps; no gradient and no dropout in level 1; then released at the scheduled lr).
**Grid, fixed now (6 cells x 10 seeds x 3 epochs; no cell added after results are seen):**
| cell | init | peak lr | schedule | level-1 lr factor | level 1 frozen |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| R1_dec_lr1e-3 | warm | 1e-3 | linear decay | 1 | no |
| R2_dec_lr5e-4 | warm | 5e-4 | linear decay | 1 | no |
| R3_const_lr3e-4 | warm | 3e-4 | constant | 1 | no |
| R4_dec_l1x0.25 | warm | 1e-3 | linear decay | 0.25 | no |
| R5_dec_freeze1ep | warm | 1e-3 | linear decay | 1 | first 706 steps |
| R6_dec_scratch | scratch | 1e-3 | linear decay | 1 | no |
Candidates = 6 cells x epoch {1, 2, 3} x weights {raw, EMA} = **36** (Stage 2 had 12 for A): more chances to pick a lucky one on validation; this is stated next to the result. R6 checks whether the warm start is still needed once the lr decays.
**Selection (validation only; `baseline/twolevel_select2.py`, committed before any April logit is opened):** collapse guard (any seed with validation AUC < 0.6 excludes the candidate); best = highest mean over 10 seeds of validation pAUC@1e-2; SE = std (ddof 1) / sqrt(10); tied = within 1 SE; among tied: fewest epochs, then lower peak lr, then raw before EMA, then scratch before warm, then fewer extra mechanisms (decay, level-1 factor, freeze). Validation did not separate the models in Stage 2 (all about 0.94 to 0.96 while April is 0.62 to 0.71), so the validation choice may not carry over to April; this is a limit, not something corrected here.
**Control B.** B is not re-tuned over this grid. After the choice, B is trained ONCE more with the chosen schedule, peak lr, EMA flag and epoch (no padding, scratch; the warm start, level-1 factor and freeze do not apply to B) as **B2**, 10 seeds, so that A2 vs B2 is a like-for-like comparison of padding; the old A and B (19.12) stay as they are. B2 is not selected on anything else.
**April, once.** The chosen A2 (and B2) are evaluated on April once with the same code as 19.13 (`twolevel_eval`), against A, B, ATT+bal and v2-PE, with the same claim rule (stratified and cluster bootstrap). The April logits of all cells are stored and not opened before the choice is committed; the other cells go into an appendix "not used for selection". If A2 is chosen, its attack results (the same attack scripts) are run for it too; the attack results of A (19.13) are not replaced. No April number is used for any choice.
**Estimate (T4, measured: 129 s per seed-epoch for A, plus about 15 s for the EMA evaluation and storing):** 6 cells x 10 seeds x 3 epochs = 180 seed-epochs x 145 s = **about 7.3 GPU-h** (about 3.7 h of wall time on two kernels of two T4); B2 about 0.4 GPU-h; attacks for A2 about 14 GPU-h at the Stage 3 size (random, surrogate: about 1.3 GPU-h; white-box seeds 0-2: about 4.5 GPU-h). The owner said not to worry about the GPU limit; the quota used so far is reported in the final text.

**19.14 Stage 3: first attack results (2026-11-20; kernels `nebula-2l-s3-scores` and `nebula-2l-s3-wba` both COMPLETE; scores in `results/attbal_2l/attack_scores/`, tables and figure in `results/attbal_2l/attacks/`; white-box of B and v2-PE still running in `nebula-2l-s3-wbb`, queued).** Checks passed in every process: the unedited scores equal the training run's April logits (max difference 0.0 for A and B, all 10 seeds); ATT+bal and v2-PE reproduce their stored random-padding scores for k = 5, 20, 50 exactly (difference 0.0), so the edits are the same as in REPORT 5.10/5.11. Measured: scoring about 12 min per model (all 23 edit sets x 10 seeds), white-box about 18 min per seed for A with early stopping (the early stop of evaded reports cut the estimate of 19.13 by a large factor).
Numbers at 1% FPR (malware flagged %, mean over 10 seeds; padded-benign FPR in the tables):
| edit | v2-PE | ATT+bal | B | **A** |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| random 5 prepend | 54.4 | 79.0 | 61.8 | 79.8 |
| random 50 prepend | 8.3 | 67.6 | 28.2 | 71.0 |
| random 100 prepend | 6.6 | 7.9 | 12.5 | 14.7 |
| random 200 prepend | 6.6 | 8.0 | 4.8 | 10.6 |
| random 100 insert | 28.1 | 61.7 | 20.0 | 65.7 |
| random 200 insert | 17.1 | 53.0 | 8.0 | 56.0 |
| LightGBM-chosen 100 prepend | 3.2 | 5.0 | 8.0 | 9.2 |
| LightGBM-chosen 100 insert | 12.4 | 46.4 | 8.6 | 47.2 |
Reading (descriptive; differences are tested in the tables with stratified and cluster bootstrap): (1) **Balanced padding is what gives robustness, not the two-level reading**: B (no padding) is far below A and ATT+bal for k >= 50 (A - B +40 to +48 points for inserts, resolved under both bootstraps). (2) **A is a few points above ATT+bal for k = 50 to 200** (+2 to +7 points, resolved under both bootstraps; for example random 100 prepend +6.8, 9/10 seeds) and far above v2-PE for inserts. (3) **But prepending 100 or 200 calls breaks all four models** (A 14.7% and 10.6%): the added calls are beyond the k <= 50 of the padded training, and A does not "catch what padding pushes out of the first 512 tokens": 100 prepended calls are about 1,000 tokens, well inside A's 4,096, and A still collapses, so the loss is not only a window effect. (4) White-box (seeds 0-2, 1% FPR, share of 500 still flagged): A 61.5% after 20 steps against 53.3% for ATT+bal (+8.2 points, 3/3 seeds, stratified CI excludes 0, cluster CI [+2.3, +15.6]); both fall to a few percent by step 50 (A 4.4%, ATT+bal 1.8%). At 0.1% FPR A is flagged less often than ATT+bal early (A's clean TPR@0.1% is lower: 42.5 vs 58.3). (5) The call lists chosen by the attack raise the padded-benign FPR of A (1% threshold: 2.1% at k = 20, against 0.3% for ATT+bal).
Not yet in: white-box of B and v2-PE (queued), the family/coinminer view under attack, the appendix of all grid cells, README and the REPRODUCTION_REPORT section (written after round 2, NOTES 20, so that they describe the final models).

**20.1 Round-2 selection result (2026-11-20; kernels `nebula-2l-r2a` and `nebula-2l-r2b` both COMPLETE; the 6 cells x 10 seeds x 3 epochs trained without a failed unit; only seed JSONs, validation logits and logs were downloaded; no April logit has been opened; `results/attbal_2l/round2/`).** Validation only, rule of 20 / `baseline/twolevel_select2.py`: no candidate collapsed (the lowest validation AUC of any seed is 0.9942). Best: **R5_dec_freeze1ep, epoch 1, EMA** (mean val pAUC@1e-2 0.9530, SE 0.0028). Four candidates are within 1 SE of it (R1 epoch 1 EMA 0.9516, R4 epoch 1 EMA 0.9502, R5 epoch 1 raw 0.9503, R5 epoch 1 EMA). All are epoch 1 with peak lr 1e-3; the next tie-break (raw before EMA) gives **chosen A2 = R5_dec_freeze1ep, epoch 1, raw weights** (0.9503, SE 0.0045; the Stage 2 choice A was 0.9438, SE 0.0051). The selection spread is small (best 0.9530 vs A 0.9438 is under 2 SE), and 36 candidates were compared, so this is a validation choice with the usual winner's-curse risk.
Two things the reader must know about what A2 is: (1) **at epoch 1 of R5 level 1 is still frozen** (the first 706 steps), so A2 is the warm-started ATT+bal level-1 encoder, unchanged, plus a level 2 trained for one epoch at a lr of 1e-3 falling to 0.67e-3; level 1 never adapts to the 4,096-token reading. That is a legitimate result of the pre-registered rule, but it means "A2 vs A" is mostly "frozen pre-trained level 1 vs jointly trained level 1". (2) For the same reason **the B2 control is not exactly like A2**: by the plan (20, "Control B") B2 is trained from scratch without padding at the chosen peak lr 1e-3, linear decay over 3 epochs, raw weights, checkpoint after epoch 1; the warm start and the freeze do not apply to B. A2 vs B2 therefore still tests padding, but also differs in the level-1 initialisation. The other cells' numbers (epoch 3: 0.93 to 0.947 raw, 0.9333 to 0.9471 EMA) show that decay and EMA mainly remove the one-bad-epoch problem of Stage 2 (e.g. R1 epoch 2 raw 0.8546 against EMA 0.9186), and that scratch with decay (R6 epoch 3 EMA 0.9471) now matches the warm start.
Next: train B2 (10 seeds, 1 epoch checkpoint of a 3-epoch decay schedule; about 0.1 GPU-h of training per epoch, run the full 3-epoch schedule so that the lr at the end of epoch 1 is the same as in A2's run), then the one April evaluation of A2 and B2.

**19.15 White-box attack of B and v2-PE finished (kernel `nebula-2l-s3-wbb`, seeds 0-2, COMPLETE; scores in `results/attbal_2l/attack_scores/`, tables regenerated in `results/attbal_2l/attacks/`).** Share of the 500 malware reports still flagged at 1% FPR (mean of 3 seeds), k = 0 / 5 / 20 / 50 prepended calls chosen by the attack: v2-PE 75.6 / 9.0 / 2.2 / 1.1; B (no padding) 71.6 / 39.9 / 16.7 / 1.6; ATT+bal 77.7 / 65.5 / 53.3 / 1.8; A 78.7 / 68.5 / 61.5 / 4.4. So under the white-box attack the order is the same as under random padding (padded models far above v2-PE and B up to about 20 steps), and **every model, A included, falls to a few percent by 50 steps**: the padded training raises the number of steps the attacker needs (about 20 to 30 instead of 5), it does not stop the attack. The comparison tables (stratified and cluster CI) are in `attack_tables.md`.

**20.2 Round-2 clean April result (2026-11-20; ONE April evaluation of the chosen A2 = R5_dec_freeze1ep epoch 1 raw and of B2 = `B2_dec_scratch` (scratch, no padding, peak lr 1e-3, linear decay, epoch 1, raw), 10 seeds each; kernel `nebula-2l-b2` COMPLETE; same code as 19.13, `python -m baseline.twolevel_eval ... --new-a-dir ... --new-b-dir ...`; `results/attbal_2l/clean_round2/`).**
pAUC@1e-2, mean ± std over 10 seeds: **A2 0.699 ± 0.019**, B2 0.627 ± 0.046, A (19.12) 0.687 ± 0.017, B 0.616 ± 0.062, ATT+bal 0.707 ± 0.017, v2-PE 0.705 ± 0.031 (10-seed ensembles: A2 0.732, B2 0.694, A 0.736, ATT+bal 0.734, v2-PE 0.768). TPR@1e-3: A2 0.554, ATT+bal 0.572, v2-PE 0.543. On the validation slice A2 is 0.950 (SE 0.0045, as selected).
Claim rule on pAUC@1e-2 (points; stratified bootstrap, seeds with the same sign, cluster bootstrap):
- **A2 − A: +1.2 [−0.9, +3.5], 6/10 seeds: not resolved.** The recipe change did not produce a gain that the statistics can separate from seed noise.
- **A2 − ATT+bal: −0.8**; the stratified CI excludes 0 and 8/10 seeds are below (the claim rule is met on the stratified bootstrap), the cluster CI [−1.9, +0.07] contains 0 (not resolved). The gap to ATT+bal fell from 2.0 points (A) to 0.8 points (A2), but the sign is still the same and A2 is not above ATT+bal.
- A2 − v2-PE: −0.6 [−6.5, +3.7], 4/10 seeds above 0: not resolved (as for A).
- **A2 − B2: +7.2 [+0.06, +13.5], 10/10 seeds: meets the rule under both bootstraps**: with the same recipe, padded training is clearly better than unpadded on April clean data (B: A − B was +7.1 points in 19.13). B2 is below v2-PE (−7.8) and ATT+bal (−8.0), both meeting the rule.
- B2 − B: +1.1, not resolved.
Reading: (1) the recipe changes did not make a better clean result in a way that can be claimed; the improvement on the validation slice (0.9438 to 0.9503, under 2 SE) did not carry over beyond +1.2 points on April, as warned in 20. (2) What did change is the stability: the epoch-1 checkpoint of a decayed schedule has no bad seed (seed spread of A2 ± 0.019 against ± 0.017 for A, i.e. unchanged), so the spread over seeds is not mainly caused by the bad-epoch events but by the seeds themselves. (3) A2 is the frozen ATT+bal level-1 encoder plus a trained level 2 (see 20.1), so the result says: **jointly training level 1 on 4,096 tokens did not help over keeping the ATT+bal 512-token encoder fixed.** The two-level reading has not been shown to help on clean data here; its value (if any) is in the attack tables.
Next: the appendix with all cells (April, not used for selection), the attack scores for A2 (same scripts as Stage 3), README and the REPRODUCTION_REPORT section.

**20.3 Appendix: April result of all 36 round-2 candidates (not used for any choice; `results/attbal_2l/round2/appendix_april.md`, `baseline/twolevel_appendix.py`).** April pAUC@1e-2 (mean of 10 seeds) lies between 0.627 and 0.701 for every candidate; **none reaches ATT+bal (0.707) or v2-PE (0.705)**. The chosen A2 (0.699) is among the best of the 36 (best: R6 scratch epoch 3 EMA 0.701, raw 0.700), so the validation choice did not pick a bad candidate here, but the validation differences between the top candidates (0.945 to 0.953) are not visible on April either. Pattern across cells: epoch-2 checkpoints (lr still high in the decayed run) are the worst (0.63 to 0.68, with the largest spread, up to ± 0.098), the EMA weights are as good as the raw weights or better (-0.001 to +0.05, largest at the bad epochs), and the end of the schedule (epoch 3) gives 0.678 to 0.701 whichever cell is used. In short: lr decay and EMA make the run safe to stop at the end (no cell has a collapsed epoch 3), but they do not move the clean April result above about 0.70. Attack scores for A2 are running (kernels `nebula-2l-r2-scores` and `nebula-2l-r2-wb`, pushed 18:42 UTC; estimate 1.5 to 2 GPU-h quota).

**20.4 Attacks on A2 (kernels `nebula-2l-r2-scores` and `nebula-2l-r2-wb` COMPLETE, 2026-11-20; the unedited scores equal A2's April logits, max difference 0.0 for all 10 seeds; scores in `results/attbal_2l/attack_scores/`, tables and figure regenerated in `results/attbal_2l/attacks/`; white-box seeds 0-2).** Malware flagged at 1% FPR (%, mean of 10 seeds), A2 against A and ATT+bal: random 5 prepend 78.6 / 79.8 / 79.0; random 50 prepend 67.9 / 71.0 / 67.6; random 100 prepend 9.2 / 14.7 / 7.9; random 200 prepend 7.8 / 10.6 / 8.0; random 100 insert 60.7 / 65.7 / 61.7; random 200 insert 51.2 / 56.0 / 53.0. Paired comparisons: **A2 is below A** for k = 50 to 100 (−3.1 prepend 50, −5.5 prepend 100, −5.0 insert 100; resolved under both bootstraps); **A2 against ATT+bal is within about ±1.3 points** (prepend 100 +1.3, insert 100 −1.0, prepend 50 +0.25, mostly not resolved or resolved only in one direction by one of the two bootstraps), and **A2 is far above v2-PE** (+33 points for insert 100) and above the unpadded B (+40 points; B2 was not attacked, so the padded-versus-unpadded point under attack is made with the round-1 B). White-box (share of the 500 still flagged after 20 steps, 1% FPR, 3 seeds): A2 58.2, A 61.5, ATT+bal 53.3; after 50 steps A2 6.2, A 4.4, ATT+bal 1.8 (all models fail). The padded-benign FPR under the attack lists rises with the number of steps for A2 (1.0, 1.3, 2.1, 2.8% at k = 5, 10, 20, 50) more than for ATT+bal (0.5, 0.3, 0.3, 0.1%) and A (0.8, 1.0, 2.1, 0.8%).
Reading: the round-2 recipe did not improve robustness either; A2 is not better than A under attack (slightly worse for k = 50 to 100) and not different from ATT+bal. Prepending 100 or more calls still breaks it (9.2%, 7.8%), as for A, B and ATT+bal. The two-level model gives no resolved advantage over ATT+bal, on clean data (20.2) or under attack (19.14, 20.4), in this setting.

**20.5 Final state (2026-11-20).** Written: `results/attbal_2l/README.md` (plain English), `REPRODUCTION_REPORT.md` 5.14, loss curves (`results/attbal_2l/loss_curves/`), appendix of all round-2 cells. Kaggle use of this task, from the kernel logs (wall time): pilot (small, log not kept), Stage 2 100 + 101 min, Stage 3 scores 50 + white-box 33 + 16 min, round 2 113 + 107 min, B2 37 min, A2 attacks 38 + 33 min = about 10.5 h wall for the kernels with a log; with the charging seen in 11.8 (both T4 counted, about 2 x wall) this is **about 21 GPU-hours of quota plus the pilot**. Open points: no attack on B2 (not needed for any claim); the failure at 100 or more prepended calls (training padding stopped at k = 50) is not tested: training with k up to 200 is the obvious next experiment and was not run; the reading of "A2 = frozen ATT+bal encoder" is by construction of the selected checkpoint, not a separate test (cell R6, scratch, epoch 3 EMA gives 0.701 on April without the frozen encoder, also not above ATT+bal).

## 21. Group-split validation slice: no API sequence is shared between training and validation (plan written 2026-11-21, BEFORE any code or any split was computed; requested by the owner)

**Why.** The old validation slice (`gap.val_slice`, hash `b18269395891ee1e51d69e4a7293f0af`; called "old validation slice" from here on) is a stratified RANDOM 10% of the 75,298 January reports. January has only 6,771 distinct API-name sequences (`hash_apis`) and a few huge groups (the largest has 4,312 reports), so most validation reports have an exact twin in the training part: validation measures memorisation, it scored 0.94 to 0.96 pAUC@1e-2 while April scores 0.6 to 0.7, and it did not separate models (19.5, 20). Also, the BPE-50k tokenizer of all earlier models was fit on ALL of January, the old validation slice included (11.7). This section replaces the slice by a group split, refits the tokenizer on the new training part, and redoes the validation-based choices. The old-slice results stay valid as "old validation slice" results and are not changed.

**Stage A (CPU only): build the split. Decided now:**
1. *Groups.* Group = `hash_apis` (from `results/misses/reports.csv`, joined to our January rows by `results/padding/row_map.npz`). **Stricter grouping (the one used):** union-find over two relations: same `hash_apis`, and identical first 512 tokens (tokens of our existing BPE-50k, from the call-token store `all.npz` of the dataset `nebula-tune-inputs`; the first 512 tokens of the report = what v2-PE and ATT+bal read). The grouping uses the OLD tokenizer because the split must exist before the new tokenizer can be fit on its training part; **afterwards the first-512 identity is re-checked with the NEW tokenizer and the assertion "no first-512 token sequence occurs in both parts" must hold; if it does not, the groups are rebuilt from the union of both tokenisations and the split is recomputed before anything else is done** (this loop is allowed because it uses no model result).
2. *Split.* `sklearn.model_selection.StratifiedGroupKFold(n_splits=10, shuffle=True, random_state=2026)`, labels = the 9 family labels of `results/hier/data/meta.csv` (clean, windows_syswow64 and the 7 malware families), groups = the stricter groups; **fold 0 (the first fold the iterator yields) is the validation slice, fixed now**. Acceptance (fixed now): validation share of the reports between 8% and 12%, benign and every one of the 8 malware families present with at least 20 validation reports (windows_syswow64, 235 reports in January, is exempt from the 20 and only has to appear). If an acceptance condition fails I report it and ask; I do not try other folds or seeds.
3. *Checks (written to `results/groupsplit/README.md`):* (a) groups shared by train and validation = 0; (b) share of validation reports whose `hash_apis` occurs in the training part = 0, and for comparison the same share for the old slice and for April (April against the new training part); (c) near-duplicates: share of validation reports with a training report within normalised edit distance <= 0.05 on the API-name sequence (the sequence of calls of the filtered report, i.e. what the model reads, from the call store; distance = Levenshtein / max(length); computed exactly with `rapidfuzz` between DISTINCT sequences with the length filter |la - lb| <= 0.05 max(la, lb), then weighted by report counts), the same for April against the new training part and for the old slice against its old training part; (d) counts per family in training / validation / April, size of the 10 largest groups, number of groups per side; (e) the old tokenizer-based and new-tokenizer-based first-512 check.
4. *Saved:* `results/groupsplit/split.npz` (train and validation indices into our January rows 0..75,297, sorted), `split_hash.json` with the sha256 (first 32 hex digits) of `train.astype(int64).tobytes() + val.astype(int64).tobytes()` exactly as for the old slice (the same construction as `gap.val_slice`); `baseline/groupsplit.py` provides `val_slice()` and **asserts that hash in every later run**.
5. *Tokenizer.* A new BPE-50k is fit on the new TRAINING part only (no validation, no April) with exactly the settings of `preprocess.py` variant `bpe50k` (authors' `JSONTokenizerBPE`, vocab 50,000, seq 512, authors' cleaning and stop words, pinned nebula commit), by a CPU kernel on the raw reports (same mechanism as `nebula-b3-tokens`, 53 min for bpe8k_v2; `tok_fit_idx` = the new training indices), which then re-tokenises all 92,700 reports (`x_train`, `x_test`) and builds the call-token store with the new tokenizer (`all_gs.npz`, same builder as `callstore.py`, original reports only; nothing edited is saved). Stored as new files (private dataset `nebula-groupsplit`); the old tokenizer files (`baseline_models/tokenizers/bpe50k`, `nebula-tune-inputs`) are not touched. Reported: pieces of the old and new vocabularies that differ, the share of first-512 tokens (as piece strings) that differ between old and new tokenisation, and the share of reports whose first 512 tokens are not identical in the two tokenisations.
Stop after Stage A and show the README. **Nothing is committed or pushed until the owner has reviewed each stage.**

**Stage B (GPU, small; only after the owner approves A): choices on the new validation slice. Grid and rule are written here and completed with the GPU estimate (and the exact commands) BEFORE training; no April logit is opened.**
- v2-PE (paper PE, flatten): lr in {1e-3, 2e-3, 3e-3}, epochs 1 to 4, 5 seeds (0-4).
- ATT+bal: lr in {1e-3, 2e-3} x padding probability p in {0.25, 0.5}, epochs 1 to 4, 5 seeds; robustness R on the validation slice as in NOTES 16.3, now with 20 AND 100 prepended calls (the definition of R is copied from 16.3 into the Stage B text; R is computed for the 100-call edit as for the 20-call edit).
- Selection: highest mean validation pAUC@1e-2 over the seeds; candidates within 1 SE (SE = std ddof 1 / sqrt(5)) of the best are tied; among tied, fewer epochs, then lower mean validation loss; for ATT+bal candidates whose R (each of the two, 20 and 100) is more than 2 points below the best R are excluded first (before the tie rule). Collapse guard as before (a seed with validation AUC < 0.6 excludes the candidate).
- Logged per epoch: training loss and validation loss, same format as `results/loss_curves`. Report the chosen settings and compare them with the old ones (lr 2e-3, 2 epochs, p 0.5).
**Stage C (only after the owner approves B):** v2-PE and ATT+bal with the chosen settings, 10 seeds (0-9), on the new training part; ONE clean April evaluation per model (pAUC@1e-2 primary, TPR@1e-2, TPR@1e-3, AUC, F1, per family, the coinminer cluster; claim rule of EVAL_PROTOCOL 5 with the stratified AND the cluster bootstrap); loss-curve figure (training vs validation loss) for both. **No attacks** (a separate task). Outputs: `results/groupsplit/` (README in plain English, tables, figures), a new section of REPRODUCTION_REPORT.md, this section.
**Rules:** the Kaggle token is never printed, pasted or committed; datasets and kernels stay private; edited reports exist in memory only (only scores are saved); no April number is used for any choice; no commit or push before the owner has reviewed the stage. (The merge of `origin/ccr-1ac1816e-y66901` into `nebula-baseline` was a fast-forward without conflicts, done locally, not pushed.)

### 21.1 Amendment to Stage A item 1 (added 2026-11-21, after the first split run, before any model was trained)
The first run used the two relations of item 1 (same `hash_apis`, same first 512 old-BPE tokens). Its acceptance rules held, but 0.87% of the validation reports had an identical FILTERED CALL-NAME SEQUENCE in training (same calls, different `hash_apis`, because the hash is taken over a different serialisation of the same calls). That is an exact twin for the model, so a **third relation was added: same filtered call-name sequence** (from the call store). Groups are now the union-find closure of the three relations (6,771 `hash_apis` groups -> 6,695 groups). The split was recomputed once with the same rule (fold 0, seed 2026); no other fold or seed was used to choose anything. The three relations are listed in `split_hash.json` (`keys`). No model result was involved. Item 1's loop "rebuild the groups if the NEW tokenizer finds a shared first-512 sequence" was checked: 0 shared (below), so no rebuild.

### 21.2 Stage A result for FOLD 0 (2026-11-21; CPU only; nothing trained; nothing committed) -- SUPERSEDED by 21.3 and 21.4: fold 0 was rejected; the numbers are kept as the record of the first attempt (outputs archived outside the repo)
- **Split** (fold 0): training 68,407, validation 6,891 (9.15%), hash `7b32b16914e7481535d90cb2f7e0e6b1`. Groups shared 0; validation reports whose `hash_apis` is in training 0%; first-512 sequences shared, old tokenizer 0, **new tokenizer 0** (`results/groupsplit/tokenizer_compare.json`). Acceptance rules (item 2): all met (9.15% in 8-12%; every family >= 20; windows_syswow64 present with 15).
- **Near-duplicates within 5% edit distance of a training report:** new validation 66.0%, April 64.5%, old validation 96.1% (exact twins: 0%, 35.2% (April), 94.3% (old)).
- **Balance warning (reported, nothing changed):** fold 0 is dominated by few groups: 58 groups in validation, the largest holds 51.7% of the validation reports (a backdoor+trojan group of 3,564), clean is 19% of the validation reports (old slice 32%), ransomware only 273. Validation pAUC will therefore be noisy and shaped by one group. Other folds are more balanced (e.g. 3, 5); I did NOT pick another fold because item 2 fixes fold 0. The owner decides whether to keep fold 0; a different fold needs the tokenizer kernel to be run again (66 min CPU).
- **Tokenizer:** BPE-50k refit on the 68,407 training reports only (kernel `nebula-gs-tokens`, CPU, 66 min); checks inside the kernel: labels equal old, `n_calls` equal old for all 92,700 reports, stored first-512 vs `x_*` rows differing 0, full tokenisation vs pieces differing 0 of 3,000. Old vs new vocabulary: 40,670 pieces in both, 9,331 only old, 9,331 only new; 47.8% of reports have identical first 512 tokens, 73.0% of token positions hold the same piece, median total tokens 1061 for both, mean change 2.1%. Old tokenizer files untouched.

### 21.3 Fold choice rule (owner's instruction of 2026-11-21; written BEFORE the rule was applied; no model has been trained or scored on any fold; no April number is used)
**Why fold 0 is rejected.** Its largest group is 52% of the validation slice (58 groups in all), benign is 19% of it (January: 32.8%), and three families are under 300 validation reports (ransomware 273, keylogger 192, rat 166). Validation pAUC on it would be dominated by one group. (It met the acceptance rules of item 2, which did not look at balance.)
**Rule (fixed now, applied to the ten folds of the SAME `StratifiedGroupKFold(10, shuffle, 2026)` run, data statistics only):**
1. *Candidates:* folds where every family has >= 300 validation reports AND the benign share of the slice is within 5 percentage points of January's. Reading fixed here: "family" = the 8 families clean, backdoor, coinminer, dropper, keylogger, ransomware, rat, trojan; `windows_syswow64` (235 reports in all of January) cannot reach 300 and is exempt (it only has to appear, as in item 2). "Benign" = clean + windows_syswow64 (the `benign` column of the fold table of the README). January's benign share is computed from the data: 24,668 / 75,298 = 32.8% (the owner's "about 34%" is rounded; the 5-point window is therefore 27.8% to 37.8%).
2. *Among candidates:* the fold with the smallest largest-group share of the slice; ties -> more groups; then the lower fold number.
3. If no fold is a candidate: stop and show the table of all ten folds.
4. The chosen fold becomes the validation slice, its complement the training part; the hash is recomputed; the tokenizer is refit on the new training part (the old fold-0 tokenizer outputs are kept in the scratchpad only, not used); all Stage A checks (21 item 3, item 5) are repeated and the README is rewritten. The first-512 and call-name groups stay the ones of 21.1 (old tokenizer); the NEW-tokenizer first-512 check is repeated and must give 0 shared sequences, otherwise the groups are rebuilt per item 1.
Stage B is NOT started; stop for review after step 4.

### 21.4 Stage A result for the chosen fold (2026-11-21; CPU only; no model trained or scored; no April number used for any choice; nothing committed)
- **Rule applied** (21.3, data statistics only): candidates = folds 4 and 9 (the other eight fail "every family >= 300" and/or the benign window 27.8% to 37.8%; fold 3 and 5 fail on families, folds 6, 7, 8 on benign share and families). **Chosen: fold 9** (largest group 12.1% of the slice; fold 4: 31%).
- **Split** (fold 9): training 66,580, validation 8,718 (11.58%; inside the 8-12% acceptance of item 2), hash `c3a2c99bbf5dc6ddc60697e7b9468bf8` (asserted by `groupsplit.val_slice()`; `split_hash.json` now records `fold: 9`). Groups: 5,491 train + 1,204 validation = 6,695. Shared groups 0; validation reports whose `hash_apis` is in training 0%; first-512 sequences shared: old tokenizer 0, **new tokenizer 0**.
- **Validation slice:** benign 36.3% (January 32.8%); family counts backdoor 1,039, clean 3,119, coinminer 683, dropper 821, keylogger 608, ransomware 998, rat 542, trojan 866, windows_syswow64 42. Largest group 1,056 reports (rat 542 + trojan 494), 12.1% of the slice; so most rat validation reports come from one group.
- **Near-duplicates** within normalised edit distance: <= 1%: new validation 16.7%, April 43.2%; <= 5%: new validation 56.6%, April 63.4% (old validation 94.7% and 96.1%).
- **Tokenizer** (kernel `nebula-gs-tokens`, CPU, 66.0 min, dataset `nebula-groupsplit-in` with the fold-9 split; the log shows "split hash asserted: 66580 train, 8718 validation" and `tokenizer_fit_reports` = 66,580): in-kernel checks passed (labels equal old; `n_calls` equal old for all 92,700; stored first-512 vs `x_*` rows differing 0; full tokenisation vs pieces differing 0 of 3,000). Old vs new vocabulary: 41,799 pieces in both, 8,202 only old, 8,202 only new; 47.8% of reports have identical first 512 tokens; 72.3% of token positions hold the same piece; median total tokens 1061 for both, mean change +0.28%. Old tokenizer files untouched.
- Stage B has NOT been started. The files that Stage B will read are `results/groupsplit/split.npz` + `split_hash.json` (small, in the repo) and the new tokens/store (`variants/bpe50k_gs/*`, `all_gs.npz`, large, to be published as a private dataset only after the owner approves Stage A).

### 21.5 Stage B plan: the validation-based choices redone on the group-split slice (written 2026-11-21, BEFORE any Stage B training; the owner approved Stage A; nothing is pushed)
**Data.** Split = fold 9 of 21.3/21.4 (hash `c3a2c99bbf5dc6ddc60697e7b9468bf8`, asserted by `groupsplit.val_slice()` in every run: 66,580 training, 8,718 validation = 3,161 benign (3,119 clean + 42 windows_syswow64) + 5,557 malware). Tokens = the refit tokenizer (`variants/bpe50k_gs`), call store = `all_gs.npz`, both in the private dataset `manishsinghxdev/nebula-groupsplit` (with `labels.npz`, `split.npz`, `split_hash.json`). The TRAIN pool of the padding = the 21,507 benign reports of the new training part. April is not read: every run is made with `--loss-log`, which writes no April logit and no weights.
**Fixed for every run (unchanged from 13.4 and 16.1):** paper PE, batch 96, weight decay 1e-2, clip 0.5, dropout 0.3, AdamW with a constant learning rate (no scheduler, so epoch k of a 4-epoch run is the state of a k-epoch run), the authors' `ModelTrainer`, one `fit(epochs=1)` per epoch with padding / one `fit(epochs=4)` without, seeds 0-4, 4 epochs. v2-PE: flatten pooling, no padding. ATT+bal: attention pooling and balanced padding that REPLACES the original report (k ~ U{1..50}, insert or prepend 50/50, calls from the TRAIN pool, stream `default_rng([7000, seed, epoch, row])`, new arrays every epoch and seed; a report is edited iff the first random number u of its stream is < p).
**Grid (fixed now; nothing is added after results are seen).** v2-PE: lr in {1e-3, 2e-3, 3e-3}; 3 cells x epochs 1-4 = **12 candidates**. ATT+bal: lr in {1e-3, 2e-3} x p in {0.25, 0.5}; 4 cells x epochs 1-4 = **16 candidates**. 5 seeds (0-4) per cell: 15 + 20 = 35 runs. The old settings (v2-PE lr 2e-3, 2 epochs; ATT+bal lr 2e-3, p 0.5, 2 epochs) are candidates of the grid, trained again on the new slice (so their numbers are "old setting, new slice", not the old results).
**Validation measures (group-split slice only).** Selection metric: validation pAUC@1e-2 (normalised, from logits, `common.pauc`). Also stored after every epoch: AUC, TPR@1e-2, TPR@1e-3, validation loss (BCE of the logits), training loss. **Robustness R (copied from 16.3, now with two edits):** R_k = share of the 5,557 validation MALWARE reports that are still flagged after k calls are PREPENDED, at the validation 1% FPR threshold; threshold = the benign validation logit at rank floor(0.01 x 3,161) = 31 in descending order, of the SAME model and epoch on the unedited validation reports (flagged = logit above it); the k calls of every malware report are drawn from the TRAIN pool by `default_rng([8200, row])` (per call a uniform TRAIN-pool report, then a uniform call of it), the same fixed draw for every cell, seed and epoch; the 100-call edit uses the same stream, so its first 20 calls are the 20 calls of the 20-call edit (checked on the real store); reports without calls also receive the calls; built in memory from the store, nothing written. **k = 20 and k = 100**; with 100 prepended calls most of the 512 tokens the model reads are added calls. R is computed for ATT+bal only (v2-PE has no padding training; its R is not part of any rule).
**Per-family validation numbers (descriptive only, no part of any selection).** For each of the 7 malware families: pAUC@1e-2 of ALL validation benign reports against that family's validation reports, and the share of the family above the overall 1% FPR threshold; mean and SD over the 5 seeds, for the chosen candidate and for the old setting (computed from the stored validation logits of every seed and epoch by `baseline/gs_select.py`). **Rat comes from a single group on this slice** (all 542 rat validation reports are in one group of 1,056 reports, rat 542 + trojan 494), so the rat numbers are one group, not 542 independent reports; the same caution applies to every family that is dominated by few groups (the families' group counts are in `results/groupsplit/split.npz`).
**Selection rule (fixed now; `baseline/gs_select.py`, unit-tested):** SE = std over the 5 seeds (ddof 1) / sqrt(5). (1) Collapse guard: a candidate in which any seed has validation AUC < 0.6 is excluded. (2) ATT+bal only: for R20 and for R100 separately, best R = the highest mean R among the candidates that passed (1); a candidate whose mean R20 or mean R100 is more than 2 points (0.02 absolute) below that best is excluded. (3) Among the rest, `best` = highest mean validation pAUC@1e-2; every candidate whose mean is within one SE of `best` (the SE of `best`) is tied; among tied candidates: fewer epochs, then lower mean validation loss, then the lower learning rate. The selection is made per model (v2-PE, ATT+bal) separately.
**Loss logging.** Per run (`--loss-log`) `<tag>/seed<k>.json` and `loss_curves/<tag>/seed<k>.json` in the format of `results/loss_curves`: the mean BCE of the last 10 training batches every 10 batches (dropout on; for ATT+bal on the padded mix of that epoch), per epoch the mean training loss, the loss on a fixed 2,000-report training subset (unedited, eval mode) and the validation loss, validation pAUC@1e-2 and AUC, and for ATT+bal R20 and R100. `gs_select.py` writes the table of every candidate (training loss and validation loss per epoch) and a figure per model.
**Report.** The chosen settings, their comparison with the old settings (validation numbers; old setting retrained on the new slice), the tables of all candidates and the per-family numbers, in `results/groupsplit/stage_b/` and as NOTES 21.6; then STOP for the owner's review. Stage C is not started and nothing is pushed.
**Order and commands.** (0) CPU smoke kernels `nebula-gsb-smoke-ab` and `nebula-gsb-smoke-v2` (CPU, 400 training reports, 2 epochs, no GPU quota): the driver loads the new split and tokens, asserts the hash, trains, writes R20/R100 and the loss curves. (1) After the owner has seen the estimate and says go: two GPU kernels in parallel (the account allows two GPU sessions; each kernel has 2 x T4):
```
python baseline/kaggle/build_kernel.py --slug nebula-gsb-attbal --job gs_grid --args "--family attbal --out /kaggle/working/out --split-dir split --pad-store all_gs.npz" --datasets manishsinghxdev/nebula-groupsplit --gpu
python baseline/kaggle/build_kernel.py --slug nebula-gsb-v2pe   --job gs_grid --args "--family v2pe   --out /kaggle/working/out --split-dir split" --datasets manishsinghxdev/nebula-groupsplit --gpu
kaggle kernels push -p baseline/kaggle/jobs/nebula-gsb-attbal --accelerator NvidiaTeslaT4
kaggle kernels push -p baseline/kaggle/jobs/nebula-gsb-v2pe   --accelerator NvidiaTeslaT4
kaggle kernels output manishsinghxdev/nebula-gsb-attbal -p <dir>/attbal     # and nebula-gsb-v2pe -> <dir>/v2pe (validation JSON, val logits, loss curves only; the two outputs are merged into one runs folder)
python -m baseline.gs_select --runs <merged runs dir> --out results/groupsplit/stage_b
```
Each run inside `gs_grid` is `python -m baseline.v2pe --epochs 4 --variant bpe50k_gs --split-dir split --loss-log --pe-mode paper --lr <lr> --tag <tag> --seed <s> --device cuda` plus `--pooling flatten` (v2-PE) or `--pooling attn --pad-store all_gs.npz --pad-mode replace --pad-labels all --pad-stream 7000 --pad-prob <p> --val-robust --val-robust-k 20 100` (ATT+bal); `python -m baseline.gs_grid --family attbal --dry-run` prints all 20 commands, `--family v2pe --dry-run` all 15.
**GPU estimate (before launching).** Measured earlier on the same code and the same size of training part (67,768 reports; here 66,580): ATT+bal training = 237 s (p 0.25) to 276 s (p 0.5) per seed for 4 epochs (65 s per epoch at p 0.5, edit generation included), v2-PE about 55-60 s per epoch (10 seeds x 2 epochs took 10 min on 2 x T4, 0.18 h of quota). Added here: process start, data and store loading, the two padded validation sets and their evaluation (about 1.5 min per run), evaluation of 8,718 validation reports (16% more than before), the loss logging. So: ATT+bal about 6 min per run x 20 runs = 120 run-min, v2-PE about 5.5 min per run x 15 runs = 82 run-min; two runs at a time per kernel (2 x T4): **ATT+bal kernel about 60 min, v2-PE kernel about 41 min** (plus about 5 min of kernel start-up each) => **about 1.8 h of GPU quota in total (range 1.5-2.4 h), about 65-70 min of wall time when the two kernels run in parallel.** Quota on 2026-11-21: 0.00 h used of 30 h (resets 2026-11-28). The CPU smoke kernels use none.
**Step 0 result (2026-11-21, CPU smoke kernels `nebula-gsb-smoke-ab` and `nebula-gsb-smoke-v2`, 400 training reports, 2 epochs, both COMPLETE, no GPU quota used):** the driver loaded the new split (`--split-dir`, hash assertion passed), the refit tokens `bpe50k_gs` and `all_gs.npz` from the dataset `nebula-groupsplit`; the call store has 92,700 reports; both padded validation sets were built (20 and 100 prepended calls); `val_robust_tpr` and `val_robust100_tpr` are written per epoch, the loss-curve file has the training loss, validation loss, clean training-subset loss and R20/R100 per epoch, and no April logit or weights were written. (The numbers of these 400-report runs mean nothing.) `baseline/gs_select.py` was tested end to end on synthetic run files for all 28 candidates (tables, per-family numbers, figures, selection); its rule is unit-tested (`baseline/tests/test_groupsplit.py`, 6 tests pass). **No GPU kernel has been launched; waiting for the owner to see the estimate above and say go.**

### 21.5.1 Amendment to 21.5 (owner's instruction of 2026-11-21, written BEFORE any Stage B training; it replaces the epoch range and the estimate of 21.5 for ATT+bal)
- **ATT+bal is trained for 6 epochs: candidates = lr {1e-3, 2e-3} x p {0.25, 0.5} x epochs 1-6 = 24 candidates** (21.5 said 16). **v2-PE stays at epochs 1-4 (12 candidates)** (it overfits after epoch 2 in every earlier run). Seeds 0-4, 35 runs in all. The constant learning rate (no scheduler) makes epoch k of a 6-epoch run the state of a k-epoch run, and the padding stream `default_rng([7000, seed, epoch, row])` is defined for every epoch, so epochs 1-4 of the new runs are the same as in the 4-epoch plan. `baseline/gs_grid.py` now defaults to 6 epochs for `--family attbal` and 4 for `--family v2pe`; the ATT+bal command is unchanged otherwise (`python -m baseline.gs_grid --family attbal --dry-run` prints the 20 commands with `--epochs 6`).
- **Selection rule: unchanged** (collapse guard; for ATT+bal R20 and R100 each at most 2 points below the best R; best mean validation pAUC@1e-2; ties within 1 SE of best -> fewer epochs, then lower validation loss, then lower lr). With more epochs available the "fewer epochs" tie-break is what keeps a late epoch from being chosen on noise.
- **Review material:** for the chosen ATT+bal cell the review shows the per-epoch training loss, the clean training-subset loss and the validation loss for ALL epochs 1-6 (not only up to the chosen epoch) with mean ± SD over the seeds, the batch-level training curve, and R20, R100 and validation pAUC@1e-2 per epoch (`baseline/gs_select.py` writes `fig_loss_chosen_cell_attbal.png` and the table into `validation_tables.md`; the same for v2-PE, epochs 1-4); plus the loss table and a figure of all 24 (12) candidates.
- **GPU estimate, updated.** ATT+bal run: training 65 s per epoch at p 0.5 and 57 s at p 0.25 (measured in 16.8: 262 s / 227 s for 4 epochs) => 6 epochs = 390 s / 342 s, mean 6.1 min; plus about 1.5 min of loading and set-up and about 0.6 min more for the two padded validation sets and the 8,718-report evaluation after each of 6 epochs => **about 8.2 min per run x 20 runs = 164 run-min; two runs at a time on the 2 x T4 of the kernel => about 82 min + 5 min start-up = about 87 min.** v2-PE unchanged: 15 runs x 5.5 min = 82 run-min => about 41 + 5 = 46 min. **Total about 2.2 h of GPU quota (range 1.9 to 2.9 h; was 1.8 h), about 90 min of wall time with the two kernels in parallel.** Quota on 2026-11-21: 0.00 h used of 30 h. (The owner's instruction to update the estimate "before launching" is read as: update, then launch; the kernels are pushed right after this entry is committed locally.)

### 21.6 Stage B result (2026-11-21; VALIDATION numbers only; no April logit exists for these runs; nothing is pushed)
**Runs.** 35 runs (v2-PE 15 x 4 epochs, ATT+bal 20 x 6 epochs), all exit 0, every run asserted the split hash and trained on 66,580 reports with 8,718 validation reports (3,161 benign) and the tokenizer `bpe50k_gs`; kernels `nebula-gsb-attbal` (66.0 min) and `nebula-gsb-v2pe` (26.5 min). **GPU quota used: 1.57 h** (estimate 2.2 h, range 1.9-2.9; the runs were faster than estimated: 371 s of training per ATT+bal run of 6 epochs, 182 s per v2-PE run of 4 epochs). Files: `results/groupsplit/stage_b/` (`validation_tables.md` all 12 + 24 candidates, per-family numbers, loss tables; `selection.json`; `fig_loss_curves_*.png`, `fig_loss_chosen_cell_*.png`; `runs/` per-seed JSON and loss curves). The validation logits stay in the scratchpad (not in the repo).
**Selection by the rule of 21.5/21.5.1 (applied unchanged):**
- **v2-PE: lr 2e-3, epoch 1** (mean val pAUC@1e-2 0.4796, SE 0.0068; the runner-up (1e-3, epoch 1) has 0.4712, which is below best minus 1 SE = 0.4728, so nothing is tied). Old setting (2e-3, epoch 2) on the new slice: 0.4546.
- **ATT+bal: lr 1e-3, p 0.5, epoch 6** (mean val pAUC@1e-2 0.4641, SE 0.0094; R20 0.482, R100 0.144). Old setting (2e-3, 0.5, epoch 2) on the new slice: 0.4622, R20 0.458, R100 0.087 (excluded by the R rule).
**What the numbers say, and two problems the owner must see before Stage C:**
1. **The new slice does not separate the settings.** Mean validation pAUC@1e-2 is 0.43-0.48 for v2-PE and 0.41-0.49 for ATT+bal (the old slice: 0.94-0.96); the SE of a candidate is 0.005-0.04, so the differences between most candidates are inside the noise. v2-PE's "best" beats the next candidate by 0.8 points (one SE). Per family (descriptive only): backdoor 0.83-0.89, ransomware 0.85-0.87, dropper 0.66-0.73 are found; trojan 0.22-0.25 and coinminer 0.06-0.17 are mostly missed; **keylogger (0.00-0.02) and rat (0.00) are not found at all** at the 1% FPR threshold (rat is one group on this slice). AUC is still 0.86-0.93 at the minimum over seeds, i.e. the ranking is fine but not at the 1% FPR threshold. The validation loss rises steadily with the epoch while the training loss falls (v2-PE 1.0 -> 3.8 over epochs 1-4; ATT+bal chosen cell 0.75 -> 2.0 over epochs 1-5, 1.76 at epoch 6): the models become more and more confident on training groups and wrong on unseen groups; pAUC does not follow the loss.
2. **The R rule decided the ATT+bal choice, not pAUC.** R100 is small for every candidate (0.02-0.14; 100 prepended calls push the original report almost out of the 512 tokens) and R20 is 0.34-0.50 (bounded by the clean TPR, which is about 0.5). The rule "exclude candidates whose R20 or R100 is more than 2 points below the best R" therefore left **one** of the 24 candidates, (1e-3, 0.5, 6), whose R100 (0.144 +- 0.022 over seeds) is the highest; the next highest R100 are 0.114, 0.110, 0.105. The rule as written in 21.5 was applied; it was written when R was around 0.95 and a 2-point margin was a tight band, here it is larger than the whole spread of R100. For information only (post-hoc, not part of the rule, not used): without the R step the rule would choose **(1e-3, 0.25, 2)** (val pAUC 0.4900, SE 0.0068, R20 0.452, R100 0.052), the best pAUC of all 24 candidates; its R100 is the lowest group of the grid. The owner decides whether to keep the rule's result or change the rule; nothing has been changed here.
**Status:** Stage B is complete; **Stage C is not started.** Waiting for the owner's review of the selection.

### 21.7 Amendment to the ATT+bal selection rule (owner's decision of 2026-11-21; made AFTER the Stage B validation results of 21.6 were seen; no April data have been seen; written and committed BEFORE the amended rule is applied)
**What was seen first.** The rule of 21.5 (R20 and R100 each at most 2 points below the best) left one of the 24 ATT+bal candidates, because R100 is 0.02 to 0.14 for every candidate; 21.6 reports this and, for information, what the rule would choose without the R step. So this amendment is a change made with knowledge of the validation results, and it is recorded as such; it is not a pre-registered rule.
**Amendment (owner's decision).**
- The robustness exclusion uses **R20 only**: a candidate is excluded if its mean R20 is more than 2 points (0.02 absolute) below the best mean R20 (best = highest mean R20 among the candidates that pass the AUC guard). This is the rule of NOTES 16 (20 prepended calls).
- **Reason:** R100 measures padding beyond the training range (the training padding adds at most 50 calls); all candidates fail it (mean 0.02-0.14), so a 2-point margin on it selects on noise. The R100 condition was added for Stage B without calibration.
- **R100 is reported for every candidate as descriptive only** (the column stays in `validation_tables.md`; it takes no part in any selection).
- **Everything else is unchanged:** collapse guard (any seed with validation AUC < 0.6 excludes the candidate), highest mean validation pAUC@1e-2, ties within 1 SE of best (SE of best, 5 seeds, ddof 1) -> fewer epochs, then lower mean validation loss, then the lower learning rate.
- **v2-PE:** the rule's choice of 21.6 is accepted (lr 2e-3, epoch 1); nothing changes for v2-PE.
**Implementation.** `baseline.gs_select.select(rows, with_robust, robust_keys=("mean_r20",))`; the first plan is still available as `robust_keys=("mean_r20", "mean_r100")`. Unit test updated (`baseline/tests/test_groupsplit.py`, 6 tests pass). The outputs of the first rule are kept next to the new ones (`validation_tables_rule_21.5.md`, `selection_rule_21.5.json`).
**Report to the owner after applying:** the chosen ATT+bal candidate, and R20 and R100 of the chosen cell and of the pAUC-best cell (best mean pAUC among all 24 candidates). Stage C is NOT started and waits for the owner's confirmation. Nothing is pushed.

### 21.8 Stage B result under the amended rule of 21.7 (2026-11-21; VALIDATION numbers only; nothing pushed; Stage C NOT started)
- **ATT+bal chosen: lr 1e-3, p 0.5, epoch 3** (mean val pAUC@1e-2 0.4799, SE 0.0086; val loss 1.119; R20 0.478; R100 0.083). Best mean R20 over the candidates = 0.4965 ((1e-3, 0.5, 4)), so the R20 rule excludes mean R20 < 0.4765; 5 candidates survive: (1e-3, 0.5, 3), (1e-3, 0.5, 4), (1e-3, 0.5, 5), (1e-3, 0.5, 6), (2e-3, 0.5, 4); the best of them by pAUC is (1e-3, 0.5, 3); the tied ones within 1 SE (>= 0.4713) are (2e-3, 0.5, 4) 0.4752 and (1e-3, 0.5, 4) 0.4736, and the chosen one already has the fewest epochs. **The chosen candidate is a close call on R20:** it is 1.85 points below the best R20, the margin is 2.0.
- **Highest mean validation pAUC@1e-2 over all 24 candidates (the "pAUC-best" cell): (1e-3, 0.25, 2)** = 0.4900 (SE 0.0068), val loss 0.662, **R20 0.452, R100 0.052**; excluded by the R20 rule (4.5 points below the best R20).
- **Old setting on the new slice (lr 2e-3, p 0.5, epoch 2):** 0.4622, R20 0.458, R100 0.087 (excluded, 3.8 points below the best R20).
- The whole differences between these candidates are inside the noise of 5 seeds (SE 0.006-0.014); the per-epoch loss curves of the chosen cell (epochs 1-6) and of the pAUC-top cell are in `results/groupsplit/stage_b/fig_loss_chosen_cell_attbal.png`, `fig_loss_pAUC-top_cell_attbal.png` and the tables of `validation_tables.md`; the outputs of the first rule are kept in `validation_tables_rule_21.5.md` and `selection_rule_21.5.json`.
- **v2-PE: lr 2e-3, epoch 1** (accepted, unchanged).
- Stage C waits for the owner's confirmation.

### 21.9 Owner's approval of Stage B, push, and the account rule (2026-11-21)
- **Approved settings (owner, after 21.8):** ATT+bal lr 1e-3, p 0.5, epoch 3; v2-PE lr 2e-3, epoch 1.
- **Pushed before any Stage C kernel:** `nebula-baseline` pushed to origin at 2026-11-21 ~07:44 UTC, head `3014fd1` (committed 07:38 UTC; Stage A, Stage B, 21.7 amendment and 21.8 result), so the commit times show that the settings were fixed before April was scored.
- **Accounts.** Stage B (and the Stage A tokenizer CPU kernel) ran on the Kaggle account `manishsinghxdev` (kernels `nebula-gsb-attbal`, `nebula-gsb-v2pe`, `nebula-gs-tokens`; datasets `nebula-groupsplit`, `nebula-groupsplit-in`), not on `msingh9365`. **From now on only `msingh9365` is used** (`/tmp/claude-0/k9.sh` runs a command only after `kaggle config view` shows the user `msingh9365`, and sets `KAGGLE_OWNER=msingh9365`; the token is never printed). Quota check on msingh9365 before the rerun: GPU 0.00 h used of 30 h (resets 2026-11-28).
- **Stage B is rerun on msingh9365** (same grid, same 5 seeds, same epochs 1-6 / 1-4, same code and data; the data are the files of the Stage A tokenizer kernel, re-published as the private dataset `msingh9365/nebula-groupsplit`): kernels `msingh9365/nebula-gsb-attbal` and `msingh9365/nebula-gsb-v2pe`, estimate as in 21.5.1 (about 1.6 h of quota, measured on the first run; about 70 min wall). The selection of 21.7/21.8 is applied unchanged to the rerun and compared with the first run; every difference is reported. Stage C is not started before that comparison.
