Models: the two new ones and, next to them, the existing old-split models (v2-PE REPORT 5.9 lr 2e-3 epoch 2 and ATT+bal REPORT 5.10 lr 2e-3, p 0.5, epoch 2; old validation slice, tokenizer fit on all of January). Metrics: pAUC@1e-2 (primary), TPR@1e-2, TPR@1e-3, AUC, F1, pAUC@1e-3; per seed and as 10-seed ensemble; validation numbers of the new models; claim rule of EVAL_PROTOCOL 5 (CI excludes 0 and the same sign in >= 7 of 10 seeds) with the stratified bootstrap AND the cluster bootstrap over the 2,509 April hash_apis groups (1,000 resamples, numpy seed 0) for the pairs new ATT+bal - new v2-PE (the claim), new v2-PE - old v2-PE, new ATT+bal - old ATT+bal, old ATT+bal - old v2-PE; per-family detection rate at each model's 1% FPR threshold; the coinminer cluster (hash_apis d91b96b4..., 265 reports); figure of training vs validation loss per epoch and per batch for both models (`fig_loss_curves_train_vs_validation.png`). Note for reading: the old models and the new ones differ in split, tokenizer, epochs and seeds; the old-vs-new differences are not a clean test of one factor.
- **No attacks** (separate task). Stage C is committed locally and NOT pushed until the owner has reviewed it.

Stage C: ATT+bal 10 runs = 5 per GPU = about 33 min + 5 min start-up + the April prediction and weights (under 1 min per run); v2-PE 10 runs = about 18 min + 5 min start-up. **About 1.0 h of GPU quota in total (range 0.9-1.3 h), about 40 min wall with the two kernels in parallel.** Quota used so far this week on manishsinghxdev: 1.57 h of 30 h.

### 21.12 Stage C result (2026-11-21; ONE clean April evaluation per model; committed locally, NOT pushed; no attacks)
- **Runs and accounts.** v2-PE: kernel `manishsinghxdev/nebula-gsf-v2pe` (16.5 min, 10 runs). ATT+bal: kernel `manishsinghxdev/nebula-gsf-attbal` (queued about 1 h 50 min, then 40.3 min, 10 runs); it completed first, so by the rule of 21.11 these are the evaluated ATT+bal runs. The copy `msingh9365/nebula-gsf-attbal` (pushed 09:47 UTC at the owner's request) never ran: its status is CANCEL_ACKNOWLEDGED; its output was never downloaded or opened. The dataset `msingh9365/nebula-groupsplit` exists (private, same files). GPU quota: manishsinghxdev 2.55 h used of 30 h this week (Stage B 1.57 h + Stage C about 1.0 h), msingh9365 0.00 h. The Stage B rerun on msingh9365 was not done (owner's instruction, 21.10). `baseline/gs_eval.py` asserted: approved settings = `stage_b/selection.json`, every run asserted the split hash and the 66,580/8,718 sizes, April logits exist for the chosen epoch ONLY (epoch 1 for v2-PE, epoch 3 for ATT+bal).
- **Clean April, pAUC@1e-2 (primary), mean ± std over 10 seeds (10-seed ensemble):** new v2-PE (lr 2e-3, epoch 1) **0.634 ± 0.024 (0.689)**; new ATT+bal (lr 1e-3, p 0.5, epoch 3) **0.590 ± 0.052 (0.647)**; old-split v2-PE 0.705 ± 0.031 (0.768); old-split ATT+bal 0.707 ± 0.017 (0.734). TPR@1e-2: 0.732, 0.665, 0.811, 0.795; TPR@1e-3: 0.479, 0.503, 0.543, 0.572; AUC 0.9599, 0.9386, 0.9644, 0.9713; F1 0.880, 0.860, 0.915, 0.913 (same order). Validation slice (group split, 8,718): v2-PE pAUC 0.484 ± 0.026, ATT+bal 0.470 ± 0.019.
- **Claim rule (pAUC@1e-2, CI of the per-seed means excludes 0 and >= 7 of 10 seeds with the same sign):** new ATT+bal − new v2-PE **−4.4 points**, stratified bootstrap CI [−6.5, −2.2], 7/10 seeds negative: **resolved (ATT+bal worse)**; cluster bootstrap over 2,509 April groups: CI [−9.4, +0.8], **not resolved**. TPR@1e-3 +2.4 [−6.0, +8.1] (no difference). New v2-PE − old v2-PE **−7.1** [−8.3, −5.8], 10/10 seeds, cluster CI [−14.9, −1.5]: **resolved (worse) under both bootstraps**. New ATT+bal − old ATT+bal **−11.7** [−13.6, −9.6], 10/10 seeds, cluster [−19.7, −4.6]: **resolved (worse) under both**. Old ATT+bal − old v2-PE +0.2: not resolved (as in 5.10).
- **Per family (detection at the 1% FPR threshold, %, new v2-PE / new ATT+bal vs old v2-PE / old ATT+bal):** keylogger 70.3 / 63.4 vs 87.1 / 87.0; rat 76.5 / 69.8 vs 89.2 / 87.8; trojan 61.7 / 62.4 vs 80.9 / 80.4; ransomware 68.2 / 61.7 vs 70.8 / 72.9; coinminer 76.7 / 61.7 vs 81.7 / 72.8; backdoor 81.5 / 76.3 vs 83.7 / 82.1; dropper 73.7 / 78.7 vs 80.1 / 81.9; false-positive rate on clean 1.0% for all. **Coinminer cluster (265 reports):** flagged by 7/10 new v2-PE seeds (median margin +1.6) and 0/10 new ATT+bal seeds (−6.6); old: 8/10 and 1/10.
- **Loss curves** (`stage_c/fig_loss_curves_train_vs_validation.png`): training loss falls (v2-PE 0.20 -> 0.05, ATT+bal 0.16 -> 0.04) while the validation loss on the group-split slice rises at every epoch (v2-PE 1.0 -> 3.8 over epochs 1-4; ATT+bal 0.86 -> 1.7 over epochs 1-6), with wide seed bands; the chosen epochs are the earliest of the grid ranges (v2-PE epoch 1, ATT+bal epoch 3).
- **What this does and does not show.** The models trained on the group-split training part score 7 to 12 points lower on April pAUC@1e-2 than the old-split models (resolved, all 10 seeds). The comparison changes several things at once: the split (whole groups removed from training), the tokenizer (refit on the training part), the settings (fewer epochs, ATT+bal lr 1e-3) and the seeds; this experiment cannot say which one costs the points. The largest drops are in keylogger, rat and trojan, the families that lose whole groups to the validation slice; that is a hypothesis, not tested here. No old-split model was selected with a split that matches April; the old validation slice rewarded memorisation (21), the new one has noisy per-family numbers (21.6). A model trained on the full January set with the new tokenizer, or the new split with the old epochs, would separate the factors; neither was run.
- Files: `results/groupsplit/stage_c/` (`tables.md`, `per_model.json`, `bootstrap.json`, `cluster.json`, `family.json`, `fig_loss_curves_train_vs_validation.png`, `logits_new_models.npz` April and validation scores of the 20 new models, `runs/` per-seed JSON and loss curves, `README.md`); weights of the 20 new models are in the kernel outputs (not in the repo).

## 22. Stage D: how much of the drop of the new-split models is training too long, and which factor is left? (plan written 2026-11-21 BEFORE any Stage D computation; requested by the owner after 21.12)
**Context.** On April, the models trained on the group-split training part score lower than the old-split ones (pAUC@1e-2: v2-PE 0.634 vs 0.705, ATT+bal 0.590 vs 0.707; 10 seeds each); new and old differ in split, tokenizer, epochs, ATT+bal learning rate and seeds, and the validation loss of the new models rises at every epoch while the training loss falls (21.12). Stage D changes ONE factor at a time and changes nothing that is not listed here. Nothing is pushed; commits are local; no attacks; Kaggle account `manishsinghxdev`.
**What exists (checked 2026-11-21).** Per-epoch weights were NOT saved: Stage C stored weights and April logits only for the chosen epoch (v2-PE epoch 1, ATT+bal epoch 3). **Per-epoch validation logits WERE saved for all 10 seeds and all epochs** (v2-PE epochs 1-4, ATT+bal epochs 1-6; `seed<k>_epoch<e>_val_logits.npy`, in the kernel outputs, copied to the scratchpad). So Step 1 needs no training; Step 2 does (April scores at other epochs do not exist).
**Step 1 (CPU, validation logits only; `baseline/gs_epochs.py`).** For every seed and every epoch of both models: validation pAUC@1e-2, TPR@1e-2 (threshold from the validation benign reports), AUC and validation loss recomputed from the stored validation logits (checked against the logged `val_loss`); mean ± SD over the 10 seeds; the epoch of the highest mean validation pAUC; per-seed best epochs; whether validation pAUC falls as validation loss rises (computed: change of the mean pAUC against the change of the mean loss from epoch 1 to the last epoch and rank correlation over the epoch means and over all seed-epoch points) or stays flat (overconfidence rather than worse ranking). Figure: validation pAUC per epoch next to validation loss.
**Step 2 (GPU, then CPU).** The Stage C runs are run again (same code, same seeds 0-9, same settings: v2-PE lr 2e-3 for 4 epochs, ATT+bal lr 1e-3 p 0.5 for 6 epochs, new tokenizer, new split) under the tags `gsd_v2pe` / `gsd_attbal`, with ONE addition: `--april-every` stores the April logits of every epoch (logits only; no weights). Reproduction check against Stage C (CPU): validation logits of the chosen epoch and April logits of the chosen epoch must agree with Stage C (the maximum difference is reported; earlier same-code reruns agreed exactly). **Early-stopping rule (fixed now): per seed, the epoch with the highest validation pAUC@1e-2 among all epochs of that run (v2-PE 1-4, ATT+bal 1-6); ties -> the earlier epoch.** `baseline/gs_early.py` makes the choice from the validation logits only (it never opens a `*_test_logits.npy`), writes `results/groupsplit/stage_d/early_stop_selection.json`, and that file is committed locally BEFORE any April logit of Stage D is opened. Then April, once per model: pAUC@1e-2 (mean ± std over 10 seeds), the 10-seed ensemble pAUC@1e-2, TPR@1e-2, TPR@1e-3 (plus AUC, F1, pAUC@1e-3 as in the earlier table). Comparisons with the current new-split numbers (Stage C) and the old-split numbers.
**Step 3 (new split, old epoch count; conditional).** Old models: 2 epochs. New models, 2 epochs, with the new tokenizer and every other new setting: v2-PE lr 2e-3, ATT+bal lr 1e-3 p 0.5. The learning rate is constant (no scheduler) and the data order is sequential, so epoch 2 of a Stage D run IS a 2-epoch run (this is the equivalence used for the old models too, tests/test_v2pe.py); no new training is needed: the epoch-2 April logits are already stored by the Stage D runs. **Gate (fixed now): Step 3 is scored for a model only if, in Step 2, the early-stopped model is still resolved worse than the old-split model of the same type on pAUC@1e-2 (cluster bootstrap CI of the difference excludes 0 and the difference is negative); otherwise it is not scored and the epoch-2 April logits stay unopened.**
**Step 4 (where the thresholds are set).** In every table so far, TPR@1e-2 and TPR@1e-3 are computed by the authors' `get_tpr_at_fpr`, which takes the threshold from the set being evaluated, i.e. **from the April benign reports**; the per-family detection rates and the coinminer-cluster flags use `padtest.thresholds` on the April benign scores of each model and seed; so the "1.0% clean false-positive rate" of 21.12 is TRUE BY CONSTRUCTION (the threshold is the April benign logit at rank floor(0.01 x 8,003)). pAUC is threshold-free. New table: the threshold is set on the VALIDATION slice (the benign validation logit at rank floor(0.01 x n_benign) in descending order, of the same model and seed; 3,161 benign reports for the new models, 2,467 for the old validation slice) and applied unchanged to April: April false-positive rate on benign reports, April TPR on malware, per family. For the old ATT+bal no validation logits were kept (REPORT 5.12 note), so its row is n/a; the old v2-PE has them.
**Reporting rules (owner).** One table in the format of 21.12 with a row per new condition; for every comparison both bootstrap intervals (stratified and cluster over the 2,509 April hash_apis groups, 1,000 resamples, numpy seed 0) and the number of seeds going each way; **a difference is called "resolved" ONLY if the cluster bootstrap interval excludes zero** (this is stricter than the wording of 21.12, which called the new ATT+bal - new v2-PE difference resolved on the stratified interval alone: under this wording it is not resolved); a "What this does not show" section; untested explanations marked as guesses; figures and tables in `results/groupsplit/stage_d/`, file paths listed at the end. The seeds of new and old models are different training runs, so "seeds going each way" pairs seed index i with seed index i and means only that.
**GPU estimate (before launching).** Same runs as Stage C plus one April prediction (17,402 reports, about 15-20 s) per epoch: ATT+bal 10 runs of 6 epochs = about 7.5 min per run per GPU x 5 + 5 min start-up = about 43 min; v2-PE 10 runs of 4 epochs = about 4.5 min per run per GPU x 5 + 5 min = about 28 min. **About 1.2 h of GPU quota (range 1.0-1.5 h), about 45 min wall with the two kernels in parallel.** Step 1, Step 3 scoring and Step 4 are CPU only. manishsinghxdev quota before: 2.55 h of 30 h.
**Commands.**
```
python baseline/kaggle/build_kernel.py --slug nebula-gsd-attbal --job gs_grid --args "--family attbal --stage-d --out /kaggle/working/out --split-dir split --pad-store all_gs.npz" --datasets manishsinghxdev/nebula-groupsplit --gpu
python baseline/kaggle/build_kernel.py --slug nebula-gsd-v2pe   --job gs_grid --args "--family v2pe --stage-d --out /kaggle/working/out --split-dir split" --datasets manishsinghxdev/nebula-groupsplit --gpu
kaggle kernels push -p baseline/kaggle/jobs/nebula-gsd-attbal --accelerator NvidiaTeslaT4   # and nebula-gsd-v2pe; kaggle config view checked before each push
python -m baseline.gs_epochs --runs <Stage C outputs> --out results/groupsplit/stage_d            # Step 1
python -m baseline.gs_early --runs <Stage D outputs> --out results/groupsplit/stage_d             # selection from validation logits only
python -m baseline.gs_eval_d ...                                                                   # April, Step 2 / 3 / 4 tables
```

### 22.1 Step 1 result (2026-11-21; validation logits only; no April logit opened; committed locally)
`results/groupsplit/stage_d/step1_val_per_epoch.md|json`, `fig_step1_val_pauc_vs_loss.png` (code `baseline/gs_epochs.py`; recomputed loss and pAUC agree with the logged values to 1e-15). Per-epoch checkpoints do not exist (only the chosen epoch's weights); the validation logits of every epoch and seed do, so no training was needed for this step.
- **v2-PE (epochs 1-4):** mean validation pAUC@1e-2 0.484 / 0.460 / 0.455 / 0.460 (SD over seeds 0.03 to 0.10); **peak at epoch 1** (the Stage C choice). Validation loss 0.98 -> 1.56 -> 2.32 -> 3.84, mean |logit| 15 -> 47, validation AUC 0.933 -> 0.925. Per-seed best epochs [1, 1, 3, 1, 4, 1, 3, 4, 2, 3] (spread over all four epochs).
- **ATT+bal (epochs 1-6):** mean validation pAUC@1e-2 0.467 / 0.466 / 0.470 / 0.467 / 0.463 / 0.460 (SD 0.014 to 0.019); **peak at epoch 3** (the Stage C choice, by 0.004). Validation loss 0.86 -> 1.69, mean |logit| 8.6 -> 13.9, validation AUC 0.920 -> 0.889. Per-seed best epochs [5, 2, 2, 3, 4, 5, 1, 2, 1, 3].
- **Does validation pAUC fall as validation loss rises?** Mostly no: from the first to the last epoch the mean pAUC changes by -0.025 (v2-PE, per-seed SD 0.096, 6/10 seeds lower) and -0.007 (ATT+bal, SD 0.013, 6/10 lower), i.e. within the seed noise, while the loss doubles to quadruples and the logits grow 1.6 to 3 times: the loss rise is mainly overconfidence, not a worse ranking at the 1% FPR point. The rank correlations are weak (pooled over seed-epochs -0.04 and -0.14). The full AUC does fall a little (ATT+bal -3 points, v2-PE -1), so the ranking of the bulk gets somewhat worse. The Stage C models already sit at the validation-pAUC peak, so early stopping per seed can only move them by seed noise; this predicts that Step 2 will not close a 7 to 12 point gap on April (a prediction, tested in Step 2).

### 22.2 Final-protocol decision and two added steps (owner's instruction of 2026-11-21; written and committed BEFORE any further April result is seen: the April logits of the Stage D runs (Step 2) are still unopened; Steps 0 and 5 are not run)
**Final-protocol decision (fixed now).** The final models of the thesis protocol are trained on **ALL 75,298 January reports** (group-split training part + group-split validation slice) with the settings chosen on the group-split validation slice (21.8): **v2-PE lr 2e-3, 1 epoch; ATT+bal lr 1e-3, padding probability 0.5, 3 epochs; seeds 0-9**, new tokenizer (`bpe50k_gs`, fit on the 66,580-report training part, NOT refit on all of January), and scored once on April. **Per-seed early stopping (Step 2) is diagnostic only, not the final protocol.** No validation-based choice is made in Step 5.
**Order.** Step 2 (and Steps 3 and 4 per 22) is reported first; then Step 0 (CPU), then I stop for the owner's review; then Step 5 (GPU), then I stop for the owner's review. Nothing is pushed. One Kaggle account per step, the kernel owner is written into NOTES (Step 0: no Kaggle; Step 5: `manishsinghxdev`, where the dataset `nebula-groupsplit` is). Tokens are never printed or committed; edited reports (none are used here) would exist in memory only; scores only are saved.
**Step 0 - what the split removed (CPU only; `baseline/gs_removed.py`; no model score is used to build it).**
- For **every April report**: the **nearest January report** by normalised edit distance between API-name sequences (Levenshtein / longer length, exact; the sequence = the call names of the filtered report, as in 21 item 3(c), computed between DISTINCT sequences and mapped back to reports), and **which part it is in**: new training / new validation, and for the old split old training / old validation (a distinct sequence can occur in both old parts: reported as "both"; in the new split a sequence is in exactly one part, by construction of the groups). Ties between parts are reported as ties. Also the distance to the nearest report of each part (new training, new validation, old training, old validation) and whether the April report has an exact `hash_apis` twin and in which part.
- **Per family** (7 malware families + clean + windows_syswow64): the share of April reports whose exact `hash_apis` twin is only in the new validation part, and the share whose nearest January neighbour is in the new validation part (unseen by the new models), plus the shares with a neighbour within normalised distance 0.05 and the median nearest distance, for all of January, the new training part and the old training part. **Highlighted: keylogger, rat, trojan and the largest validation group** (the group of 1,056 January reports, rat 542 + trojan 494, found by its `hash_apis`/first-512 group id in `split.npz`): how many April reports of each family have it as exact twin or as nearest neighbour.
- **Next to it, per family, the drop in detection rate at the 1% FPR threshold** between the new-split and the old-split models (v2-PE and ATT+bal separately, from `stage_c/family.json`; thresholds on the April benign scores, as in 21.12), with the rank correlation across the 7 malware families (n = 7, descriptive only; no test). Descriptive only: no explanation derived from it is claimed as tested.
**Step 5 - full-January retraining (= the final protocol; GPU; after the owner has reviewed Step 0).**
- Both models are trained on all 75,298 January reports (`--full-january`: the training indices are all January rows; the padding pool of ATT+bal = the benign reports of all of January with at least one call, the analogue of the TRAIN pool of 21.5), new tokenizer, settings above, seeds 0-9, exactly the chosen number of epochs (v2-PE 1, ATT+bal 3), April logits and weights stored after the last epoch. The group-split validation slice is part of the training set here, so the per-epoch "validation" numbers logged by the driver are in-sample and are not used for anything. No validation-based choice.
- Scored once on April with the same tables as 21.12 and Step 2: per-seed mean ± std, 10-seed ensembles, pAUC@1e-2 (primary), TPR@1e-2, TPR@1e-3, AUC, F1; both bootstraps (stratified; cluster over the 2,509 April groups), the number of seeds going each way, **resolved = the cluster interval of pAUC@1e-2 excludes 0**; comparisons: full-January vs old-split models, full-January vs new-split models (Stage C), full-January ATT+bal vs full-January v2-PE; per-family detection (April-benign thresholds, with the by-construction warning of Step 4). Thresholds set on validation are not available for these models (no held-out validation slice); said in the report. **Reported whatever the result.** What the comparison cannot separate: the full-January models differ from the old-split models in tokenizer (fit on the 66,580-report part, not on all January), epochs (v2-PE 1 vs 2), ATT+bal learning rate (1e-3 vs 2e-3) and seeds; they differ from the new-split models only in the 8,718 extra training reports.
- **GPU estimate (before launching).** From Stage C (ATT+bal 6 epochs: about 8 min per run per GPU including start-up of the run; v2-PE 4 epochs: about 3.3 min per run per GPU) and 13% more data: ATT+bal 3 epochs about 5.5-6 min per run x 5 per GPU = about 30 min + 5 min start-up = 35 min; v2-PE 1 epoch about 2.5 min per run x 5 per GPU = about 13 min + 5 = 18 min. **About 0.9 h of GPU quota (range 0.7-1.2 h), about 35 min wall with the two kernels in parallel**; manishsinghxdev quota used so far this week about 2.55 h (before Stage D) of 30 h.
- Commands (Step 5): `python baseline/kaggle/build_kernel.py --slug nebula-gsx-attbal --job gs_grid --args "--family attbal --full-january --out /kaggle/working/out --split-dir split --pad-store all_gs.npz" --datasets manishsinghxdev/nebula-groupsplit --gpu` and the same for `nebula-gsx-v2pe` (`--family v2pe --full-january`), `kaggle config view` checked before each push; evaluation `python -m baseline.gs_eval_f ...`.

### 22.3 Amendment to Step 5 of 22.2 (owner's instruction of 2026-11-21; written and committed BEFORE Step 5 is launched; Step 2 April logits are still unopened)
**The ATT+bal padding pool for the full-January models stays = the benign reports of the 66,580-report group-split TRAINING part only (the 21,507 benign reports of the training part, as in 21.5 and Stage C), NOT all of January.** This replaces the sentence of 22.2 that made the pool "the benign reports of all of January". **Reason:** the attack EVAL pool of Stage E is the benign reports of the group-split validation slice; if the full-January models were trained with those reports as padding, the model would have seen the attack calls. **Everything else in Step 5 is unchanged**: all 75,298 January reports are the training reports (the padded report replaces the original with probability 0.5 as before, including for reports of the validation slice, whose own calls are in the training set as normal reports but never serve as padding calls), new tokenizer, v2-PE lr 2e-3 for 1 epoch, ATT+bal lr 1e-3 p 0.5 for 3 epochs, seeds 0-9, April logits and weights after the last epoch, scored once on April, same tables, both bootstraps. Implementation note: `--full-january` takes the training rows from all of January but builds the padding pool with `inmem_pad.pool_rows_of(store, y, <group-split training indices>)`; the run asserts that the pool has 21,507 reports and that none of them is in the validation slice. Order unchanged: Step 2 (+ Steps 3 and 4 per the gate), then Step 0, then the owner's review, then Step 5.

### 22.7 Amendment of the final protocol (Step 5) and the diagnostic Step 3b (owner's instruction of 2026-11-21; written and committed BEFORE anything is run; no April result of this item exists; all kernels on `manishsinghxdev` unless NOTES says otherwise)
**1. Amendment of Step 5 (decided on principle, before any further April result).** The full-January final models use **a tokenizer fitted on their full training data, i.e. all 75,298 January reports**, not the `bpe50k_gs` tokenizer (fit on the 66,580-report training part). Reason: preprocessing is fitted on the final training set; the group-split refit only served to keep the validation slice unseen during selection (21). **The old BPE-50k was fitted on exactly that set with the same settings, so it is reused**: tokens `variants/bpe50k` and the call store `all.npz` of the dataset `manishsinghxdev/nebula-tune-inputs` (the files the old-split models were trained with). **Verification (read now, from the repository):** (a) same settings: `preprocess.VARIANTS["bpe50k"] = dict(kind="bpe", vocab=50000, seq=512)` is identical to `"bpe50k_gs"`, and both go through the same `run_variant` -> the authors' `JSONTokenizerBPE.train(corpus, vocab_size=50000, ...)`; (b) fit set = all of January: in `run_variant` the corpus is `events_train` (all 75,298 reports of `windows_emulation_trainset`) unless `tok_fit_idx` or `tok_train_frac < 1` is given; the old `info.json` says `trained_on_train: true` and has no subset field (the fields `tokenizer_fit_reports` and `tokenizer_train_fraction` were added later), and NOTES 11.7 records that the BPE-50k of follow-up F was fit on all 75,298 January reports, the old validation slice included; (c) no April: `events_test` is encoded only, never part of the fit corpus (`trained_on_train`); the tokenizer is fit without labels. **Direct check (CPU kernel `nebula-gstokrepro`, free of GPU quota, written now, run before Step 5):** the same fit is repeated on all 75,298 January reports with the same settings and its vocabulary and the token arrays `x_train` / `x_test` are compared with the old files of `nebula-tune-inputs` (and the new `tokenizer.model` with `baseline_models/tokenizers/bpe50k/tokenizer.model` by sha256 here). Decision fixed now: if they are identical, the old files are reused as stated; if they are not, Step 5 uses the arrays and the call store of THIS refit instead (the principle, a tokenizer fitted on all January, is what counts) and the difference is reported. **Everything else in Step 5 stays as in 22.2 and 22.3:** settings chosen on the validation slice (v2-PE lr 2e-3, 1 epoch; ATT+bal lr 1e-3, p 0.5, 3 epochs), padding pool = the benign reports of the 66,580-report group-split training part (21,507; with the old call store the same reports, old tokenization), all 75,298 reports as training rows, seeds 0-9, April logits and weights after the last epoch, scored once. **The ATT+bal epoch count is NOT changed because of the Step 3 April result: the 2-epoch April observation (22.5) is not used for any choice.** Step 5 is run after Step 3b and after the owner's review.
**2. Diagnostic Step 3b (one factor: the tokenizer).** The Stage C setup exactly (group-split training part of 66,580 reports, the same settings: v2-PE lr 2e-3, 1 epoch; ATT+bal lr 1e-3, p 0.5, 3 epochs; seeds 0-9; same recipe, same split, same padding stream) **but with the OLD tokenizer** (old `x_train` / `x_test` and old call store `all.npz` for the padding calls, both of `nebula-tune-inputs`; the split from `nebula-groupsplit`; `--variant bpe50k --pad-store all.npz --split-dir split`, tokens found by `all.npz`). The only difference to Stage C is the tokenizer (fit on all of January, incl. the validation slice, vs the 66,580 training reports). Runs go to exactly the chosen number of epochs (v2-PE 1, ATT+bal 3) with `--april-epoch` = that epoch (April logits and weights stored once; `--loss-log` stores the validation logits and losses). Scored once on April: the same table as 21.12 and 22.5 with rows for 3b, Stage C and the old models; pairs 3b - Stage C, 3b - old, Stage C - old, 3b ATT+bal - 3b v2-PE; both bootstrap intervals, seeds going each way, **resolved = the cluster interval excludes 0**; per family (April-benign thresholds, with the by-construction warning). It tells how much of the remaining ~7 points is the tokenizer. **Diagnostic only: not used for any choice.** Kernels (owner `manishsinghxdev`): `nebula-gstk-v2pe`, `nebula-gstk-attbal`; datasets `manishsinghxdev/nebula-tune-inputs` + `manishsinghxdev/nebula-groupsplit`; code `baseline/gs_grid.py --tok-old` (new flag; `v2pe.py --tokens-find`), evaluation `baseline/gs_eval_tk.py`.
**GPU estimates (before launching).** 3b: ATT+bal 3 epochs about 5.2 min per run per GPU x 5 = 26 min + 5 min start-up; v2-PE 1 epoch about 2.2 min x 5 = 11 min + 5 min: **about 0.8 h of quota (range 0.6-1.0 h), about 31 min wall with two kernels**. ## 23. Stages F, G, H, run UNATTENDED back to back (plan written 2026-11-22 BEFORE any computation of these stages; owner not available until morning; GPU cost is not a constraint; nothing is pushed)
**Names.** M1 = v2-PE, M3 = ATT+bal (final models of Step 5, 22.10: all January, old tokenizer `bpe50k`, v2-PE lr 2e-3 1 epoch, ATT+bal lr 1e-3 p 0.5 3 epochs, seeds 0-9). M2 = two-level model with NO padding (level 1 = the attention-pooled encoder of ATT+bal, level 2 as `hier_nebula`; the model B of NOTES 19). M4 = ATT+bal-2L (the same two-level model trained with balanced padding applied to the whole call list BEFORE chunking, plan of ATT+bal, p 0.5, stream 7000; the model A of NOTES 19). Implementation: `baseline/twolevel.py` (recipe unchanged: AdamW, weight decay 1e-2, clip 0.5 global norm, constant lr, effective batch 96, dropout 0.3 / 0.1, fp32, first 4,096 tokens = up to 8 chunks of 512), extended only by: `--split-dir` (group split, hash asserted, 66,580 / 8,718), `--full-january` (training rows = all 75,298, padding pool = the benign reports of the group-split training part only, 21,507, asserted disjoint from the validation slice, as 22.3), `--april-epoch`, `--warm-dir` (explicit `seed<k>.pt` files, strict load of every non-head key, sha256 recorded), `--val-robust` (R20 on the validation slice) and `--tokens-find all.npz` (old-tokenizer dataset). Tokenizer: the OLD BPE-50k everywhere (verified identical to a fit on all of January, 22.8).
**Accounts and kernels (decided now).** Stage F and Stage G: account `manishsinghxdev` (datasets `nebula-tune-inputs`, `nebula-groupsplit`, new private weight datasets); Stage H: account `msingh9365` (fresh 30 h quota; datasets re-published there). The username is checked before every launch, the kernel owner is recorded in NOTES/STATUS.md, tokens are never printed or committed. **No duplicate kernels on two accounts: a queued kernel is waited for.** Edited reports exist in memory only; scores only are saved.
**Stage F - selection on the group-split validation slice** (old tokenizer, group-split training part of 66,580 reports, seeds 0-4, 3 epochs per run; the epochs 1, 2, 3 of a run are candidates because the learning rate is constant; `--no-april`: NO April logit is produced in Stage F). Candidates: **M2** lr in {1e-3, 2e-3} x epoch 1-3 = 6 candidates (tags `fm2_lr1e-3`, `fm2_lr2e-3`); **M4** lr in {1e-3, 2e-3} x init in {scratch, warm} x epoch 1-3 = 12 candidates (tags `fm4_scratch_lr*`, `fm4_warm_lr*`; warm = the level-1 weights initialised from the Step-3b ATT+bal of the SAME seed, `gstk_attbal/seed<k>_epoch3_model.pt` of kernel `manishsinghxdev/nebula-gstk-attbal`, published as the dataset `gs-3b-attbal-weights` as `seed<k>.pt`; every non-head key must match, the head is skipped). **Rule (fixed now), per model separately:** (1) AUC guard: a candidate in which any seed has validation AUC < 0.6 is excluded; (2) highest mean validation pAUC@1e-2 over the 5 seeds = best; (3) candidates within one SE of best (SE = std over seeds, ddof 1, / sqrt(5), the SE of best) are tied; among tied: fewer epochs, then lower lr, then scratch before warm. R20 (share of validation malware still flagged after 20 prepended TRAIN-pool calls, threshold = the validation benign logit at rank floor(0.01 x n), calls `default_rng([8200, row])`) is reported, descriptive only. Also stored per epoch: validation loss, training loss, validation logits. **The selection (validation numbers only) is written to `results/groupsplit/stage_fgh/stage_f/selection.json` and committed BEFORE Stage G starts.** If every candidate of a model is excluded: STOP.
**Stage G - final models** (all 75,298 January reports, chosen settings, seeds 0-9, exactly the chosen number of epochs, April logits and weights after the last epoch only). M4 warm -> initialised from the FINAL Step-5 ATT+bal of the same seed (`gsx_attbal/seed<k>_epoch3_model.pt`, dataset `gs-final-attbal-weights`, sha256 checked against the local files). In the same kernel session the loss curves of M1 and M3 are produced by re-running their Step-5 settings with `--loss-log` (tags `gsy_*`, same seeds; their April logits must equal those of Step 5: a determinism check, opened only for that comparison). One April evaluation each (`baseline/gs_eval_gen.py --mode g`): the same tables as Step 5 (per-seed mean ± std, ensembles, TPR@1e-2, TPR@1e-3, AUC, F1, both bootstraps with the number of seeds going each way, per family, coinminer cluster) with comparisons **M2 - M1, M4 - M3, M4 - M2, M3 - M1** (resolved = the cluster interval of pAUC@1e-2 excludes 0); the old-split models and Step 5 models as reference rows. One figure with training and validation loss curves of all four final models: the validation slice is part of their training data, so the "validation" loss of a final model is in-sample and is labelled so; for a held-out loss the figure also shows the group-split-trained counterparts (Step 3b for M1 and M3, the chosen Stage F cells for M2 and M4).
**Stage H - attacks on the four final models** (M1, M3 from Step 5; M2, M4 from Stage G; 10 seeds each; `baseline/twolevel_attack.py` extended: families m1..m4, weights from the stored final models, group-split pools, LightGBM only). Thresholds: the benign logit at rank floor(0.01 n) and floor(0.001 n) of the model's unedited April benign scores, per model and seed (flagged = above). **Random padding:** k in {5, 20, 50, 100, 200}, prepend and insert, calls from the EVAL pool = the benign reports of the group-split validation slice with at least one call (never used in training: the padding pool of training is the group-split training part), edits keyed as in REPORT 5.10 (`make_pad.plan_test_row`), ALL 17,402 April reports (malware and benign, so the padded-benign FPR comes with it). **Surrogate-chosen padding:** the LightGBM API-name ranking refit on the group-split training part (`baseline/attack_rank.py --split-dir`, 300 trees, 31 leaves; representative calls from the EVAL pool), k in {20, 50, 100}, prepend and insert. **White-box greedy:** 500 random April malware (numpy seed 31), 200 random EVAL-pool candidates per step (numpy seed 41 + step, the same for every model and report), the one that lowers the model's own score most is prepended, up to 50 steps, a report at or below the 1% threshold stops; padded benign: the chosen call list of malware report i after 5, 10, 20, 50 steps is prepended to benign report i (numpy seed 32) and scored. Reports: padded-benign FPR for every attack; per-family detection under random 50 prepend; detection-vs-k figure for all four models; both thresholds. The unedited scores must reproduce the stored April logits (checked).
**Automatic checks (STOP at once, commit what exists, write STATUS.md):** (a) a run fails, or any final seed collapses (validation AUC < 0.6 in F; April AUC < 0.6 in G); (b) the split hash, the tokenizer variant or the training-set size is not the expected one (66,580 for F; 75,298 for G; `bpe50k`); (c) a warm start does not load the intended weights (strict load of all non-head keys; sha256 of the file recorded and compared with the local one); (d) the padding-pool size (21,507) or disjointness assertion fails; (e) any April logit would be opened before the Stage F selection is committed (Stage F produces none; the selection commit precedes the first download of a Stage G output); (f) a stage exceeds 3x its own GPU estimate. After every stage `results/groupsplit/stage_fgh/STATE.md` records the state (so that a resumed session knows where it is).
**GPU estimates (quota = wall of the kernels; the measured speed of the two-level models is about 130 s per seed-epoch on a T4 incl. April scoring, one-chunk models about 8x faster).** Stage F: 30 runs x 3 epochs x about 120 s = 3.0 GPU-h = about **1.6 h of quota** (one kernel, two T4); stop above 4.8 h. Stage G: M2 and M4, 10 seeds x chosen epochs (at most 3) = about 2.2 GPU-h plus the M1/M3 loss-log re-runs 0.3 GPU-h = about **1.4 h of quota**; stop above 4.2 h. Stage H: scores of 17 edit sets x 17,402 reports x 10 seeds (M2 and M4 about 0.8 GPU-h each, M1 and M3 about 0.1 each) plus white-box (two-level about 0.4 GPU-h per seed with the early stop of evaded reports, one-chunk about 0.06) = about 10-11 GPU-h = about **11 h of quota** over two concurrent kernels (about 5.5 h wall); stop above 33 h. Quota before: manishsinghxdev 4.44 h used of 30 h, msingh9365 0.36 h of 30 h (resets 2026-11-28).
**Outputs.** `results/groupsplit/stage_fgh/` (`stage_f/`, `stage_g/`, `stage_h/`, `STATE.md`, `STATUS.md`), NOTES 23.x entries per stage, local commits only. DO NOT PUSH.

TPR@1e-3: M2 0.344 ± 0.189, M4 0.461 ± 0.174 (M1 0.541, M3 0.590). AUC: M2 0.9670, M4 0.9679 (M1 0.9667, M3 0.9694).

M4 − M3 −4.73 [−7.53, −2.22; cluster −9.46, +0.88], 1/9, not resolved. M4 − M2 +10.05 [+6.88, +13.41; cluster +4.11, +16.33], 8/2, **resolved better** (padding helps the two-level model). **23.3 Stage H result (attacks on the four final models; kernel owner `msingh9365` for all: `nebula-fgh-h-scores` 36 min, `nebula-fgh-h-wb-m2` 65 min, `nebula-fgh-h-wb-m4` 95 min, `nebula-fgh-h-wb-m1m3` 31 min = about 3.8 h of quota against the estimate of 11 h, limit 33 h; all `failed: []`).** Inputs: dataset `msingh9365/gs-final-models` (the M1 and M3 weights are sha256-equal to the Step-5 weights; M2 and M4 are the Stage G weights), EVAL pool = the 3,161 benign reports of the group-split validation slice (never used in training), surrogate ranking `ranking_gs.json` (LightGBM fitted on the group-split training part). Automatic checks passed: in every job the unedited scores reproduced the stored April logits (M1 <= 3.1e-5, M2 0.0, M3 0.0, M4 0.0, tolerance 2e-3). Only scores and call-list lengths were saved; edited reports existed in memory only. Report: `baseline/gs_attack_report.py` -> `results/groupsplit/stage_fgh/stage_h/tables.md`, `results.json`, `fig_detection_vs_k_four_models.png`. Thresholds = the benign logit at rank floor(0.01 n) and floor(0.001 n) of each model's own unedited April benign scores, per seed (so the 1% and 0.1% FPR on unedited benign reports are true by construction).
- **Detection of April malware at the 1% threshold (%; unedited / random 50 prepend / random 100 prepend / random 200 insert / LightGBM 50 prepend / LightGBM 100 insert):** M1 78.0 / 9.0 / 6.5 / 19.0 / 3.8 / 14.2; M2 75.1 / 24.1 / 11.9 / 11.2 / 13.1 / 11.9; M3 79.0 / 66.4 / 6.8 / 45.4 / 36.3 / 38.5; M4 77.8 / 67.5 / 26.2 / 37.5 / 49.5 / 37.0. All 17 edit sets with both thresholds, and the padded-benign FPR of every set, are in `tables.md`.
- **White-box greedy (500 malware, 200 candidates per step, prepend; share still flagged at the 1% threshold after k = 0 / 5 / 20 / 50 steps):** M1 74.2 / 11.1 / 2.4 / 1.0; M2 72.4 / 43.7 / 18.6 / 13.4; M3 75.7 / 66.5 / 51.5 / 2.4; M4 74.4 / 59.3 / 48.1 / 15.1. At 0.1% FPR after 50 steps: M1 0.1, M2 0.0, M3 0.6, M4 1.1.
- **Padded-benign FPR:** after every attack the false-positive rate on padded benign reports stays at or below the clean 1% (0.1%) in nearly all cells (random padding of M1 stays near 0.8-1.0% at k = 5-200 prepend, with a large spread over seeds). The white-box FPR is highest for M2 (4.8 ± 10.9% at k = 50, one or two seeds drive it). So the detection drops are not bought by a higher false-positive rate on the padded benign reports.
- **Per family, random 50 prepend (1% threshold):** M1 drops to 1.5-5.8% in every family. M3 keeps 51.7-77.4% and M4 48.2-75.6%. M2 is between (3.2-42.9%).
- **Descriptive reading, no mechanism tested here:** the models trained with balanced padding (M3, M4) keep most detection under random padding of 20 and 50 calls, and also against the LightGBM-chosen padding of 20 calls; they are not robust at 100 prepended calls (M3 falls from 66.4% at k = 50 to 6.8% at k = 100; M4 from 67.5% to 26.2%) or to the greedy white-box attack with 50 steps (M3 2.4%, M4 15.1%). The cause of this cliff was not examined in this run. Insert padding is less harmful than prepend padding for every model at k >= 100 (at k = 50 the two are close for M3 and M4). The two-level models (M2, M4) do not show a robustness advantage over their one-chunk counterparts (M2 vs M1: better at small k, same at large k; M4 vs M3: same, wider spread over seeds, M4 white-box 15.1% vs 2.4% at 50 steps). The seed spread of M2 and M4 is large in every table.
- **What this does not show:** no attack on the final models was tuned or selected on April numbers (the padding pool, the ranking and the attack settings are those of NOTES 23); the white-box attack uses 500 April malware reports and 10 seeds per model (one attacked set per seed); adaptive attackers that change the program rather than prepend/insert calls, and padding drawn from the April benign reports, are not tested.

## 24. CPU-only follow-ups after Stage H (P1, P2) and pre-registration of P3 and P4 (written 2026-11-22 BEFORE any of it is run; the Stages F/G/H run is not touched; nothing is pushed)
**Order.** P1 and P2 run unattended AFTER Stage H has finished (or after a stop written to STATUS.md). CPU only, no GPU, no new April logit is opened (P2 uses stored logits only); results go into `results/groupsplit/stage_fgh/STATUS.md` and `stage_fgh/p1`, `stage_fgh/p2`. P3 and P4 are pre-registered here and NOT run: no GPU run for P3/P4 is started in this task.
**P1 - unknown pieces in our pipeline.**
- *Which id do unknown pieces get?* The two documents disagree because they describe two different files: NOTES line 37 describes the released `bpe_50000_vocab.json` (`<pad>`=0, `<unk>`=1, `<mask>`=2), REPORT 5.9a says unknown pieces get id 0, the padding id. In our pipeline every token id comes from `JSONTokenizerBPE`, i.e. from `SentencePieceProcessor.encode_as_ids` (and `callstore.piece_tokens`), so the id of an unknown piece is the SentencePiece `unk_id`. Checked now on `baseline_models/tokenizers/bpe50k/tokenizer.model`: `unk_id` = 0 (`<unk>`), `bos_id` = 1, `eos_id` = 2, `pad_id` = -1, 50,000 pieces; an unseen character is encoded as id 0 (`'abc ☃ xyz'` -> `[435, 49966, 0, 49966, 434]`); the vocabulary JSON of our tokenizer (50,001 entries) says `<pad>`=0, `<unk>`=1, `<mask>`=2 and is used only for the vocabulary size and the padding id (0). So **unknown pieces get id 0, which is also the padding id**, and REPORT 5.9a is right for our pipeline; NOTES line 37 is right about the JSON file only. This is confirmed on the data below (id 0 inside real, unpadded token sequences).
- *Measure (`baseline/gs_unk.py`; from the call-token store `all.npz`, whose tokens are the real, unpadded sequences, so a 0 is an unknown piece and never padding):* per report the number of tokens equal to 0 in the first 512 tokens and in the whole report; for January group-split training (66,580), January group-split validation (8,718) and April (17,402), malware and benign. Report: share of reports affected (at least one), median count (over all reports and over affected reports), mean, top unknown strings (the strings come from the raw reports: if any report is affected, a CPU kernel `gs_unk_strings` (no GPU) re-tokenises the cleaned text and lists the words that become `<unk>`; if no report is affected the list is empty and no kernel is run).
- **Decision rule (fixed now):** if, for ANY of the three populations and for EITHER the first 512 tokens or the whole report, the share of reports with at least one unknown token is > 1% OR the median count is > 0, then the fix is planned (not run) and merged into P3's tokenizer refit. The fix = unknown pieces get a dedicated id that is not the padding id (the unused index 50,000 of the 50,001-row embedding, verified to be unused in the refit; padding stays 0), recorded in P3's plan; otherwise no fix is planned.
**P2 - April-seen / April-unseen.** For each model, unseen = the April reports whose `hash_apis` is not in THAT model's own training set (final models M1-M4: all of January, 75,298 reports; Stage C and 3b models: the group-split training part, 66,580; the old-split models and the two-level A and B: the old training part, 67,768); seen = the others. Models (all from STORED logits): M1 and M3 (Step 5), M2 and M4 (Stage G), the old-split v2-PE and ATT+bal, 2L A and B (row 0 of the stored attack scores of NOTES 19), the Stage C models. Per subset: pAUC@1e-2 and TPR@1e-2 (threshold on the benign reports of the SUBSET; computed within the subset), mean ± std over 10 seeds, the 95% CI of the seed mean by the stratified bootstrap (reports resampled within class, 1,000 resamples, numpy seed 0) and by the cluster bootstrap (cells (`hash_apis` group, class) resampled; groups of the subset), subset sizes (reports, malware, benign, groups); pairwise differences M2 - M1, M4 - M3, M4 - M2, M3 - M1 and the old pair on seen and on unseen with both CIs and the number of seeds going each way (resolved = the cluster interval excludes 0). Descriptive; no claim beyond the data.
**P3 - address-role normalisation (pre-registered, NOT run).** *Normalisation (data-independent, fixed here):* in the filtered report text, before the authors' cleaning, every hexadecimal literal `0x[0-9a-f]+` of more than 4 hex digits is replaced by the role token `addr<L>x<T>` where L = number of hex digits and T = the leading hex digit (e.g. `0x7ffe0000` -> `addr8x7`, `0x401000` -> `addr6x4`); literals of at most 4 hex digits and all other text are unchanged. *Tokenizer:* BPE-50k refit (same settings, authors' `JSONTokenizerBPE`) on ALL 75,298 January normalised reports only, with the `<unk>` fix if P1 triggers it; all 92,700 reports re-tokenised, call-token store rebuilt (CPU kernel, about 70 min, no GPU). *CPU check before any training:* share of calls and of tokens changed, change of the total token count per report (median, quartiles) and of the number of calls inside the first 512 tokens; **length-matched control:** if the median total token count changes by more than 10%, a control normalisation is built in the same way and trained identically: every hex literal of more than 4 digits -> the single token `addr` (no role information); the effect of the roles is then read as the difference between the role model and the control, not against the unnormalised baseline. *Retraining (final protocol, as Step 5):* M1 and M3 on all of January, v2-PE lr 2e-3 1 epoch and ATT+bal lr 1e-3 p 0.5 3 epochs, seeds 0-9, padding pool = the benign reports of the group-split training part (normalised store), no validation-based choice; ONE April evaluation per model with the tables of Step 5 (both bootstraps, seeds each way, per family, coinminer cluster) against Step 5 (and the control). *GPU estimate:* M1 about 0.15 h and M3 about 0.35 h of quota per variant (as Step 5, 0.5 h in all), 1.0 h with the control; the tokenizer/store rebuild is CPU.
**P4 - window-shift augmentation (pre-registered, NOT run).** *Augmentation (fixed here):* during training, for each report and epoch, with probability 0.5 the report is replaced by the same report with its first j calls removed, j drawn uniformly from {1, ..., min(n_calls - 1, 100)} (reports with fewer than 2 calls are not shifted), so that the first 512 tokens come from a later part of the call list; for M3 the balanced padding of ATT+bal is applied first and the shift afterwards (the pad calls are part of the list); random stream `default_rng([7300, seed, epoch, row])`, shift and padding use the same store-based in-memory construction (`callstore.build_fast`), nothing is written. *Models and protocol:* M1 and M3 as in Step 5 (all of January, same settings, seeds 0-9, old tokenizer; the P3 tokenizer is NOT combined with P4), one April evaluation per model against Step 5, plus the robustness measures of Stage H (random padding k in {20, 50, 100}, prepend and insert, thresholds at 1% and 0.1% FPR) for the shifted models only if the owner asks. *GPU estimate:* about 0.5 h of quota (edit generation adds about 15 s per epoch).
**April openings.** Counted as in REPORT 5.13 (d) (13 scoring rounds up to REPORT 5.12): 14 the two-level A and B (REPORT 5.14), 15 the round-2 two-level models A2 and B2 (NOTES 20), 16 Stage C (21.12), 17 Stage D (22.5: Steps 2 to 4, one evaluation of the early-stopped and the 2-epoch logits), 18 Step 3b (22.8), 19 Step 5 (22.10); planned or running in NOTES 23: 20 Stage G (M2 and M4; M1 and M3 are the Step 5 logits, no new opening for them), 21 Stage H (the attack scores of the four final models on the 17,402 April reports); **remaining openings if P3 and P4 are run: 22 P3, 23 P4 (and 24 for the control, if it is built)**. P1 (token ids of April reports, no model score) and P2 (stored logits) are not model scorings.
**P5.** Step 5 is done (NOTES 22.10): the final models use all of January. Training on the group-split part alone cost about 8 points of April pAUC@1e-2 (Step 3b -> Step 5: +7.7 for v2-PE, +8.3 for ATT+bal, resolved), recovered when the held-out reports were added; the mechanism is not shown; **no further factor-isolation runs are made.**

**24.1 P1 and P2 results (CPU only; no GPU run, no new April logit; run after Stage H on 2026-11-22).**
- **P1, id check.** In our pipeline unknown pieces get id 0, the padding id: SentencePiece `unk_id` = 0 (`<unk>`), padding is 0 in the model (REPORT 5.9a is right for our files; NOTES line 37 describes the released vocab JSON only). A `0` in the call-token store is always an unknown piece, because the store holds the real, unpadded sequences (`baseline/gs_unk.py`, tables `stage_fgh/p1/unk_counts.md`).
- **P1, how many reports.** Share of reports with at least one unknown token (first 512 tokens / whole report): January training part 32.8% / 66.7%; January validation slice 30.7% / 60.2%; April 21.7% / 64.0%. Median count over all reports: 0 / 4 in each population (first 512 / whole report); malware first 512: 41.9% (training), 41.7% (validation), 30.4% (April); benign first 512: 13.8%, 11.5%, 11.4%. Maximum 244 (whole report). **Decision rule triggered** (share > 1% and whole-report median 4 > 0 in every population): **the fix is planned and merged into P3** (an own id 50,000 for unknown pieces, padding stays 0), NOT run.
- **P1, which strings (CPU kernel `manishsinghxdev/nebula-p1-unk-strings`, 28.5 min, `baseline/gs_unk_strings.py`, the raw reports filtered again with the authors' code; counts only, no report text saved; tables `stage_fgh/p1/unk_strings.md|json`).** The unknown piece is almost always **the single letter `j`**: 202,972 of the 203,102 unknown tokens in January (99.94%) and in April 45,765 `j` characters sit in unknown words against 45,755 unknown tokens (characters and tokens are counted differently, so the two numbers differ slightly); the character is `j` (U+006A), which the old BPE-50k has no piece for. Every cleaned word that contains a `j` becomes unknown at that letter: the most frequent are `abcdefghijklmnopqrstuvwxyz` (127,610 occurrences in 33,188 January reports), `getstockobject`, `waitforsingleobject`, `deleteobject`, `selectobject`, `getobjecta`, the month names `jan`, `jun`, `jul`, `january`, `june`, `july`, `adjusttokenprivileges`, `objectlocknotowned`. The other unknown characters are rare (accented letters such as `ì`, `ã`, `â`, and many CJK characters from wide-character strings, each in at most 117 reports). The shares from the raw-report run agree with the store: April 63.99% with at least one unknown token in both. Not examined: why `j` is missing from the vocabulary (a property of the fitted tokenizer; whether the released tokenizer has it was not checked), and whether the missing `j` matters for any score.
- **P1, consequence for P3 (a note, not a change of the pre-registration).** Because nearly all unknown tokens are one letter, the planned dedicated id for unknown pieces would mark each `j` as a separate token rather than restore the words; a refit that covers every character (SentencePiece `character_coverage` 1.0 or byte fallback) would turn the words into normal pieces. Which of the two P3 uses is the owner's decision; it is not decided here and nothing was run.
- **P2, April seen / unseen** (`baseline/gs_seen_unseen.py`; `stage_fgh/p2/seen_unseen.md|json`; stored logits only; unseen = `hash_apis` not in that model's own training set; pAUC@1e-2 and TPR@1e-2 computed inside the subset; both bootstraps; seeds each way). Final models (all January): seen subset 6,329 reports (2,914 malware, 3,415 benign, 637 groups), unseen 11,073 (6,485 / 4,588 / 1,872). pAUC@1e-2 seen / unseen: M1 0.793 ± 0.042 / 0.617 ± 0.060; M2 0.677 ± 0.126 / 0.513 ± 0.160; M3 0.834 ± 0.022 / 0.650 ± 0.017; M4 0.817 ± 0.026 / 0.585 ± 0.050. The same fall from seen to unseen is present for the Stage C models (v2-PE 0.787 / 0.574, ATT+bal 0.757 / 0.531), the old-split models (0.825 / 0.650 and 0.832 / 0.652) and the two-level A and B (0.797 / 0.638 and 0.749 / 0.558). Pairwise differences inside the final models (cluster-resolved pAUC@1e-2): M2 - M1 resolved worse on both subsets (seen -11.6, unseen -10.4); M4 - M2 resolved better on the seen subset (+14.0, 10/0 seeds) and not resolved on the unseen subset (+7.3, cluster interval -1.1 to +13.7); M4 - M3 and M3 - M1 not resolved on either subset. All full tables are in the file. Descriptive only: the seen and unseen subsets differ in malware families and in size, so a difference between them is not a causal effect of having seen the sequence.
- **P3 and P4** stay pre-registered and not run; the April openings list above already holds Stage G (20), Stage H (21), P3 (22), P4 (23).


## 25. Corrected tokenizer `bpe50k_fix` and the four final models again (Stages I and J) - pre-registered 2026-11-22 BEFORE any computation of these stages; unattended; GPU cost is not a constraint; nothing is pushed
**Why.** P1 (NOTES 24.1) showed that the old BPE-50k has no piece for the letter `j`: the letter becomes the unknown id 0 = the padding id, in 66% of all reports (median 4 per report). Attention pooling (M2, M3, M4) treats id 0 as padding, so those `j` positions are masked there. This stage replaces the tokenizer and changes nothing else. Only run before this registration: `nebula-fix-src` (manishsinghxdev, CPU, seconds) which copied the authors' tokenizer source file to read their training options; no report, score or model was touched.
**1. Tokenizer `bpe50k_fix` (CPU kernel, `baseline/gs_tokens_fix.py`).** The authors' own functions do the filtering, cleaning and training: SentencePiece BPE, vocab 50,000, the SAME input text (the cleaned `str` of ALL 75,298 January reports, the authors' `clear_json_event`, chunks of 4,192 characters; no April) and the SAME options as the old `bpe50k` (`--model_type=bpe --split_by_number=False --max_sentence_length=4192 --max_sentencepiece_length=64`), EXCEPT `--character_coverage=1.0 --byte_fallback=true --pad_id=0 --unk_id=1 --bos_id=-1 --eos_id=-1` (id 0 is reserved for padding only). Implementation: the authors' `JSONTokenizerBPE.train` is used unchanged, only its SentencePiece trainer call gets the extra flags appended (variant `bpe50k_fix` in `baseline/preprocess.py`). The authors' `load_vocab` writes `vocab.json` from the `.vocab` file (a trailing empty line gives 50,001 entries, as for the old tokenizer, so the embedding table has 50,001 rows as before, id 50,000 unused; it renames the keys at positions 0 to 2 to `<pad>`, `<unk>`, `<mask>`, which changes names only, not ids). All 92,700 reports are re-tokenised (`x_train`, `x_test` with 512 tokens, and the call-token store `all.npz`, original reports only). **CPU checks, STOP if any fails:** (a) the rows are the old rows: published labels identical, number of calls of every report equal to the old store, API-name vocabulary of the store equal to the old store (so the ranking `ranking_gs.json` of Stage H stays valid); (b) number of unknown tokens (id 1) = 0 in January and in April, in `x_train`, `x_test` and the store; (c) no real token has id 0 (id 0 occurs in the store never and in `x_*` only as right padding); `unk_id` 1, `pad_id` 0; (d) the first 512 tokens of every report in the store equal the row of `x_train` / `x_test`; the concatenation of the pieces equals the full tokenisation for 3,000 reports; (e) **edits against full re-tokenisation:** for 600 January and 600 April reports with at least one call, a prepend or an insert of k in {5, 20, 50} calls (drawn from other reports) is built in memory with `callstore.build_fast` and compared (first 512 tokens and total length) with the full tokenisation of the edited report (edited reports in memory only). **Reported (length confounder):** tokens per report (median, mean, share longer than 512) and the number of calls that lie completely inside the first 512 tokens (median, mean, share of reports with fewer than 3 calls inside), old against new, per population and class. Output: dataset `manishsinghxdev/gs-fix-inputs` (`all.npz`, `labels.npz`, `variants/bpe50k_fix/`). Estimate: CPU about 2 h (filter 24 min, fit 17 min, encode 8 min, store about 70 min); stop above 6 h.
**2. Stage I - the four final models again (account `manishsinghxdev`, GPU).** All 75,298 January reports, the new tokenizer (`--variant bpe50k_fix`, `--tokens-find all.npz`, dataset `gs-fix-inputs`), EXACTLY the final settings of NOTES 22.10 and 23.1, seeds 0-9, exactly the chosen number of epochs, April logits and weights after the last epoch only: **M1** v2-PE lr 2e-3, 1 epoch; **M2** two-level, no padding, lr 1e-3, scratch, 1 epoch; **M3** ATT+bal lr 1e-3, p 0.5, 3 epochs; **M4** ATT+bal-2L lr 1e-3, balanced padding, scratch, 1 epoch; padding pool = the 21,507 benign reports of the group-split TRAINING part (asserted, disjoint from the validation slice), stream 7000 as before. **No new selection of any kind; the tokenizer is the only change (decision fixed now), whatever the result.** Commands: `gs_grid --family v2pe --fix` and `gs_grid --family attbal --fix` (tags `gsz_v2pe`, `gsz_attbal`; the commands of the Step 5 loss-log runs `gsy_*` with `--variant bpe50k_fix`, loss logs kept for the figure) and `twolevel --plan --seeds 0..9 --epochs 1 --april-epoch 1 --split-dir split --tokens-find all.npz --variant bpe50k_fix --full-january --cells zm2:none:scratch:1e-3 zm4:balanced:scratch:1e-3`. GPU estimate (from the measured times of Step 5 and Stage G, the new store has the same number of calls): `gsz_v2pe` 0.12 h, `gsz_attbal` 0.35 h, `zm2`/`zm4` 0.46 h = about **0.95 h of quota** on two kernels in parallel; stop above 2.9 h (3x).
**3. April evaluation (opening 22, one scoring per model; `baseline/gs_eval_i.py`).** The tables of Stage G for the four fixed-tokenizer models (per-seed mean +- std, 10-seed ensembles, per family, coinminer cluster); **comparisons, both bootstraps (1,000 resamples), seeds each way, resolved = the cluster interval of pAUC@1e-2 excludes 0:** for each model the fixed against the current tokenizer (M1f - M1, M2f - M2, M3f - M3, M4f - M4; the current models are the stored Step 5 and Stage G logits, no new opening for them) and, under the fixed tokenizer, M3f - M1f, M4f - M3f, M4f - M2f. P2 seen / unseen columns for the four fixed models (stored logits, `gs_seen_unseen.py` with the new logits added). Loss curves of all four fixed models next to the current ones (`gs_fig_i.py`). Seeds are paired index with index only; no seed-level claim of pairing is made.
**4. Stage J - attacks (opening 23; account `msingh9365`, GPU).** As Stage H (NOTES 23.3) on the four fixed-tokenizer models: random padding k in {5, 20, 50, 100, 200} prepend and insert (pool = group-split validation benign, 3,161), LightGBM-chosen padding k in {20, 50, 100} (`ranking_gs.json` of Stage H, valid because the API-name vocabulary and call counts are asserted equal), white-box greedy (200 candidates per step, prepend, 50 steps, 500 April malware, 10 seeds), padded-benign FPR for every attack, thresholds at 1% and 0.1% FPR on the clean April benign scores of each model and seed, per-family detection under random 50 prepend, detection-vs-k figure. Every job asserts that the unedited scores reproduce the stored April logits of Stage I (tolerance 2e-3). Edits are rebuilt on the new tokens (check (e) above). Estimate: as Stage H, **about 3.8 h of quota**; stop above 11.4 h. Weights and logits go to the private dataset `msingh9365/gs-fix-models` (+ copies of `gs-fix-inputs`, `nebula-groupsplit` on that account).
**Automatic checks (as in NOTES 23; STOP at once, commit what exists, write STATUS.md):** a run fails or a final seed collapses (April AUC < 0.6); the split hash, the tokenizer variant (`bpe50k_fix`) or the training-set size (75,298) is not the expected one; the padding pool size (21,507) or the disjointness assertion fails; any CPU check of part 1 fails; any April logit is opened before this registration is committed; a stage exceeds 3x its GPU/CPU estimate. One account per stage (tokenizer and Stage I: manishsinghxdev; Stage J: msingh9365), the username is checked before every launch, no duplicate kernels (a queued kernel is waited for), the kernel owner is recorded, tokens are never printed or committed, edited reports exist in memory only (scores and lengths are saved), `STATUS.md` is written at the end, everything is committed locally, **nothing is pushed**.
**April openings (the list of 24 is extended; P3 and P4 are not run here):** 14 two-level A and B (REPORT 5.14), 15 round-2 models (NOTES 20), 16 Stage C, 17 Stage D, 18 Step 3b, 19 Step 5, 20 Stage G, 21 Stage H; **22 Stage I (the four fixed-tokenizer models, one scoring each), 23 Stage J (their attack scores)**; remaining if P3, P4 are run: 24 P3, 25 P4 (26 for the control). If P3 is run later, its tokenizer refit would start from `bpe50k_fix` instead of the old tokenizer with a dedicated unknown id (the planned fix of NOTES 24 is superseded by this stage; the owner decides).
**What this does not show.** Which of the changes caused any difference (the missing `j` pieces, the larger vocabulary of single characters and bytes, the different ids, a different random initialisation of the embedding rows) is not separated; the new tokenizer is fitted on all of January like the old one, so no tokenizer-specific selection on April occurs; token counts per report may change, so a difference may partly be a length effect (reported in part 1, not controlled).

**25.1 Tokenizer and Stage I results (CPU kernel `manishsinghxdev/nebula-fix-tok` 57.8 min, estimate 2 h; GPU kernels on `manishsinghxdev`: `nebula-fix-i-attbal` 19.8 min, `nebula-fix-i-2l` 27 min, `nebula-fix-i-v2pe` 6.4 min of runtime after a queue wait of about 1 h; about 0.95 h of quota against the estimate of 0.95 h).**
- **Tokenizer checks (all passed, `stage_i/checks_gs_tokens_fix.json`):** the rows are the old rows (published labels, calls per report, API-name ids and API-name vocabulary identical to the old store, so `ranking_gs.json` stays valid); unknown tokens (id 1) = 0 in January and April (store and `x` rows); no real token has id 0 (store none; `x` rows only right padding); `unk_id` 1, `pad_id` 0; first 512 tokens of every store report = the `x` row; concatenation of the pieces = full tokenisation for 3,000 reports; **1,200 of 1,200 edited reports (600 January, 600 April; prepend or insert of 5, 20 or 50 calls) built by `build_fast` equal the full re-tokenisation**; vocabulary file 50,001 entries (as before; embedding table 50,001 rows), 75,298 January reports in the fit.
- **Length (`stage_i/length_change.md`):** the corrected tokenizer gives 0.4% fewer tokens per report (median ratio new/old 0.996-0.997 in every population and class; median tokens per report January 1,063 to 1,059, April 1,014.5 to 1,007); the share of reports longer than 512 tokens is unchanged (66.5% January, 63.4% April), and the median number of calls that lie completely inside the first 512 tokens is unchanged (83 January, 80 April; mean 66.5 to 66.7). **Length is not a meaningful confounder here.**
- **A failed first launch (recorded):** `nebula-fix-i-2l` version 1 failed within 20 s at data loading because `twolevel --plan` did not pass `--variant` to its worker processes (they looked for the old tokenizer's files); no training ran, no April logit existed. The launcher was fixed (`--variant` is passed on) and the kernel relaunched as version 2 unchanged otherwise. Not treated as a stop (nothing was trained or scored); stated here and in STATUS.md.
- **Checks of the runs:** 10 seeds each, 75,298 training reports, `bpe50k_fix`, the right split hash and, for the padded models, the 21,507-report pool; no collapsed seed (lowest April AUC: M1 0.942, M2 0.955, M3 0.955, M4 0.959).
- **April pAUC@1e-2, 10 seeds (corrected tokenizer; current tokenizer in brackets; ensemble in the next entry):** M1 **0.686 ± 0.026** (0.674 ± 0.053); M2 **0.620 ± 0.080** (0.562 ± 0.146); M3 **0.705 ± 0.025** (0.710 ± 0.015); M4 **0.661 ± 0.035** (0.663 ± 0.036). TPR@1e-3: M1 0.536 (0.541), M2 0.381 (0.344), M3 0.567 (0.590), M4 0.453 (0.461). AUC: M1 0.9639, M2 0.9690, M3 0.9693, M4 0.9677 (current 0.9667, 0.9670, 0.9694, 0.9679). Ensembles (pAUC@1e-2): M1 0.756 (0.739), M2 0.698 (0.675), M3 0.730 (0.731), M4 0.719 (0.725).
- **Fixed minus current tokenizer (points, resolved = cluster interval of pAUC@1e-2 excludes 0; seeds up / down; seeds paired by index only):** M1 +1.28 [cluster -0.98, +3.79] 5/5 not resolved; **M2 +5.80 [+3.12, +8.94] 7/3 RESOLVED better**; M3 -0.50 [-1.18, +0.20] 5/5 not resolved; M4 -0.16 [-2.13, +1.60] 4/6 not resolved. **Under the fixed tokenizer:** M3 - M1 +1.84 [-3.22, +6.39] 7/3 not resolved; M4 - M3 -4.39 [-9.43, +1.14] 1/9 not resolved; M4 - M2 +4.10 [-0.62, +8.89] 6/4 not resolved (it was resolved better under the current tokenizer, +10.05).
- **Reading (descriptive):** adding the missing letter `j` does not change the picture for the padding-trained one-chunk model M3 or for M4 (differences near zero, not resolved); it raises M2 (the two-level model without padding) by about 6 points, resolved, and M1 by about 1 point (not resolved). M2's seed spread falls from SD 0.146 to 0.080. The ranking M3 > M1 > M4 > M2 of the current tokenizer becomes M3 > M1 > M4 > M2 as well, with all gaps between M1, M3 and M4 unresolved. The coinminer cluster: M1 flagged by 8/10 seeds (5/10 before), M2 9/10 (6/10), M3 1/10 (0/10), M4 9/10 (9/10). Per-family tables, the stratified intervals and the figure `fig_loss_curves_fixed_vs_current.png` are in `stage_i/`. Not shown: which part of the change (the `j` pieces, the character and byte pieces, the new ids, a new embedding initialisation) does the work.

No tokenizer-specific claim is made from these rows (only M1 white-box is missing).

The M1 scores are unchanged.

## 26. Follow-ups for the MTP report (corrected tokenizer, SET B): items 0-5 - pre-registered 2026-11-22 BEFORE any computation of items 2, 3 and 4; unattended; nothing is chosen on an April number
**Rules for all items.** Commit and push after each item; Kaggle tokens never printed or committed; one account per job and the owner recorded; no April number is used for any choice; a failed check stops the work and is written into `results/groupsplit/stage_fgh/STATUS.md`; the automatic checks of NOTES 23/25 apply to every training or attack job (split hash, tokenizer `bpe50k_fix`, training-set size 75,298 (final) or 66,580 (grid), padding pool 21,507 benign group-split training reports and disjoint from the validation slice, collapse = validation AUC < 0.6 in a grid / April AUC < 0.6 in a final seed, a failed run, 3x the GPU estimate). Edited reports exist in memory only; only scores are saved.
**Item 0 (no compute).** The F1 of the clean tables, the way seeds and the bootstrap combine, and the thresholds of the attack tables are read from the code and written into STATUS.md.
**Item 1 (CPU, stored scores).** (a) *Cluster bootstrap for the Stage J cells:* the method of the clean tables (`twolevel_eval.cluster_bootstrap`): cells (`hash_apis` group, class) of the 17,402 April reports resampled with replacement within each class, 1,000 resamples, `numpy` seed 0, ONE resample used for every model, seed and edit set of a draw; in every draw the threshold of each model and seed is recomputed on the resample (the unedited April benign logit at rank floor(fpr n), flagged = strictly above), detection = share of the resampled April malware flagged after the edit, padded-benign FPR = share of the resampled April benign reports flagged after the same edit; the interval is the 2.5/97.5 percentile of the MEAN OVER THE 10 SEEDS. The white-box rows use the same resample as multiplicities of the 500 attacked reports (and of the 500 padded benign reports). Both thresholds (1%, 0.1%), all 17 edit sets, white-box steps 5/10/20/50. (b) *Tokens and window:* median and IQR of BPE tokens per padding call under the random sampler of the attacks (a uniform EVAL-pool report, then a uniform call in it), and over all calls of the EVAL pool for reference; per model the median (and IQR) number of random padding calls needed, when prepended, to push the first token of the original report (the first token after the report head) to position >= the model's window (512 for M1 and M3, 4,096 for M2 and M4, i.e. the first malicious token no longer inside the window) for the 9,399 April malware reports with at least one call (sequential draws of the same sampler, a vectorised own stream; the tokenizer is `bpe50k_fix`); the number of April benign reports above the 1e-3 threshold of each model and seed (n_benign = 8,003, rank floor(0.001 n) = 8). Output `results/groupsplit/stage_fgh/followups/`.
**Item 2 (GPU, attack runs only).** Random padding with calls from a HELD-OUT pool: **the EVAL pool = the 3,161 benign reports of the group-split VALIDATION slice** (a January group fold that is NOT in the training padding pool, which is the 21,507 benign reports of the group-split training part of the models' training; the disjointness is asserted). Limit, stated: M1 to M4 were trained on ALL of January, so these 3,161 reports were seen as clean training examples but never as a source of padding calls; a pool unseen in every sense does not exist for the final models without using April, so the synthetic no-op control is NOT run. Edit sets: random padding, prepend and insert, k in {20, 50, 60, 75, 100}, plus the unedited set (thresholds, check); same editing code and same sampler (keyed by the April row, k and mode), same thresholds as Stage J, so the k = 20, 50, 100 sets must reproduce Stage J exactly (check: identical scores). All four models, 10 seeds. Job `baseline/gs_attack.py --ks 20 50 60 75 100 --no-surrogate`, kernel owner `msingh9365` (weights dataset `gs-fix-models`), estimate 23 min (Stage J scores: 35 min for 17 sets), stop above 1.2 h. Reported like Stage J (`stage_k/`, with cluster intervals of item 1a, detection-vs-k figure).
**Item 3 (GPU, training): the 2 x 2 (head x padding) at ONE matched budget.** Cells: flatten/none (the M1 type), attention/balanced p 0.5 (the M3 type), attention/none (a), flatten/balanced p 0.5 (b); corrected tokenizer, padding = the in-memory replace-padding of NOTES 21 (pool = the 21,507 benign group-split training reports, stream 7000, k U{1..50}, insert/prepend 50/50, both classes eligible), all other settings those of `baseline/v2pe.py` (AdamW, weight decay 1e-2, batch 96, clip 0.5, dropout 0.3, constant learning rate). **Budget selection rule (fixed now, validation only):** candidates (lr, epochs) in {1e-3, 2e-3} x {1, 2, 3} (6; the epochs 1-3 of a 3-epoch run are the candidates, constant lr); every cell is run on the group-split training part (66,580) and scored on the group-split validation slice (8,718), seeds 0-4, `--loss-log`, no April logit exists in this grid; the score of a candidate = the MEAN OVER THE FOUR CELLS of the mean validation pAUC@1e-2 (5 seeds); a candidate is excluded if any seed of any cell has validation AUC < 0.6; best = the highest score; candidates within one SE of best (SE = standard deviation, ddof 1, over the 5 per-seed scores (each the mean over the four cells), divided by sqrt(5), the SE of best) are tied; among tied: fewer epochs, then the lower lr. The result is ONE (lr, epochs) used for all four cells (it may differ from the final M1 and M3 settings; the M1 and M3 of Stage I are only a descriptive reference because their budgets differ). **Final:** the four cells trained with the chosen budget on all 75,298 January reports, seeds 0-9, April logits and weights after the last epoch only (`--april-epoch`), one April evaluation each (the tables of Stage I: per-seed mean +- std, ensembles, per family, coinminer cluster), the head and padding effects as paired differences with both bootstraps and seeds each way (attention - flatten without padding, attention - flatten with padding, balanced - none for flatten, balanced - none for attention; resolved = the cluster interval of pAUC@1e-2 excludes 0), then the attack scores (random 20 and 50 prepend, LightGBM 50 prepend, unedited; padded-benign FPR at 1% and 0.1%; EVAL pool of item 2, `ranking_gs.json`), cluster intervals as in item 1a. Account `manishsinghxdev` (grid, final training and attack scores; weights dataset `gs-2x2-models`). GPU estimates (about 1.3 GPU-min per seed-epoch in every cell, from Step 5 and Stage G): grid 120 seed-epochs = about 2.7 GPU-h = about 1.4 h of quota on one kernel, stop above 4.2 h; final at most 120 seed-epochs = about 1.4 h, stop above 4.2 h; attack scores about 0.1 h.
**Item 4 (GPU, training): malware-only padding with the final M1 and M3 configurations.** M1 type: flatten, lr 2e-3, 1 epoch; M3 type: attention, lr 1e-3, 3 epochs; both with padding applied to MALWARE training reports only (`--pad-labels malware`, p 0.5, k U{1..50}, insert/prepend 50/50, pool = the 21,507 benign group-split training reports, stream 7000, replace), corrected tokenizer, all 75,298 January reports, seeds 0-9, April logits after the last epoch. No selection (the settings are those of M1 and M3). Evaluation: padded-benign false-positive rate (and detection) at random 5, 20, 50 insert and prepend (EVAL pool, 1% and 0.1% thresholds), compared with the final M1 and M3 (descriptive), cluster intervals as in item 1a; the clean April pAUC@1e-2 of the two new models is reported descriptively from the same logits. Account `msingh9365` (training, attack scores; dataset `gs-malware-padding-models`). Estimate about 0.15 h (M1 type) + 0.4 h (M3 type) + 0.1 h scores = about 0.7 h, stop above 2.1 h.

**What this does not show.** The 2 x 2 is at one budget chosen on validation: another budget could change a cell. The held-out padding pool of item 2 is held out as a padding source only. The seed-mean intervals of item 1a describe the sampling of April reports and groups, not seed-to-seed variation (that is shown by the std over seeds and the seeds-each-way counts).

**26.1 Item 1 results (CPU, stored scores; `results/groupsplit/stage_fgh/followups/`).**
- **1a, cluster-bootstrap intervals of every cell of the Stage J tables** (`stage_j_cluster_ci.md|json`; method of NOTES 26; point values asserted equal to `stage_j/results.json`, largest difference 2e-16). Examples (detection at the 1% clean-FPR threshold, % [95% interval of the 10-seed mean], M1 / M2 / M3 / M4): unedited 79.1 [68.6, 85.8] / 77.2 [66.3, 84.1] / 79.0 [68.6, 86.3] / 77.9 [68.0, 85.2]; random 50 prepend 7.4 [4.3, 11.6] / 28.5 [14.3, 46.2] / 65.1 [52.3, 74.9] / 67.2 [55.1, 76.4]; padded-benign FPR (%), random 50 prepend 1.07 [0.23, 2.18] / 0.05 [0.01, 0.13] / 0.84 [0.50, 1.39] / 0.49 [0.38, 0.63]. The intervals are wide because the April reports form 2,509 groups and some groups are large (the unedited detection alone spans about 17 points); they describe the sampling of reports and groups, not seed-to-seed variation. **A caution for the white-box rows:** with the threshold recomputed on every resample the intervals are wide and lopsided (M1 after 20 steps 1.6 [0.7, 23.5]) because the greedy search stops as soon as a report falls below the full-set threshold, so a resample with a slightly lower threshold flags such reports again; the file therefore also gives the interval with the attack's own FIXED threshold (only the reports resampled): M1 1.6 [0.6, 3.2], M2 19.6 [12.0, 28.0], M3 48.7 [34.3, 60.7], M4 50.2 [35.2, 62.5] after 20 steps, and 0.6 [0.2, 1.1], 13.8 [7.9, 20.4], 2.5 [1.1, 4.6], 10.6 [6.4, 15.8] after 50 steps.
- **1b, tokens and window** (`tokens_per_call.md|json`, corrected tokenizer): **a padding call costs a median of 5 BPE tokens (IQR 4 to 7; mean 6.2) under the attack sampler** and 5 (IQR 4 to 6) over all 579,163 calls of the EVAL pool (min 3, max 121). The report head is short (median 1 token, IQR 1 to 15), so the first token of the original report is at about position (number of padding calls) x 5: **about 82 random calls (IQR 78 to 86) push it out of the 512-token window of M1 and M3, about 663 (IQR 652 to 674) out of the 4,096-token window of M2 and M4** (no report censored below 4,000 calls). This is the scale of the collapse of M3 and M1 between 50 and 100 prepended calls in Stage J (NOTES 23.3 / 25.2); that the window is the cause was not tested. **April benign reports above the threshold:** with 8,003 benign reports the 1e-3 threshold is the benign logit at rank 8, so 8 benign reports are above it (7 to 8 for M1, 8 for M2 to M4 over the seeds), and at 1e-2 it is rank 80 (80; 79 to 80 for M2, 77 to 80 for M4): the 0.1% rows rest on eight reports.

| edit | M1 (no padding) | M1 settings + malware-only padding | M3 (balanced padding) | M3 settings + malware-only padding |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| random 5 prepend | 0.91 ± 0.40 | **30.1 ± 13.9** | 1.03 ± 0.10 | **20.4 ± 4.2** |
| random 20 prepend | 1.29 ± 1.38 | **86.3 ± 10.2** | 1.12 ± 0.22 | **46.9 ± 6.7** |
| random 50 prepend | 1.07 ± 1.96 | **97.7 ± 1.9** | 0.84 ± 0.52 | **82.4 ± 5.9** |

Detection of April malware after the same edit (1% threshold, %): the malware-only padding models are above the final models at every k (random 50 prepend: M1 settings 98.6 against M1 7.4; M3 settings 94.2 against M3 65.1; unedited 79.3 / 78.7 against 79.1 / 79.0), but **the same edit applied to BENIGN reports is flagged as well** (the table above): with malware-only padding the models learn that padding itself is a sign of malware, so the high detection under padding is bought with false alarms on padded benign reports. Cluster 95% intervals of every cell: `item4_cluster_ci.md` (for example M3 settings + malware-only padding, random 50 prepend: false alarms 82.4 [74.2, 88.4] %, detection 94.2 [90.6, 96.3] %). Balanced padding (M3) keeps the padded-benign rate at about 0.7 to 1.1% (the clean 1%) at every k; the models without padding (M1) stay near 1% as well, with large seed spread (0.6 to 1.3%, cluster interval at 50 prepend [0.23, 2.18]). Clean performance of the malware-only models is the same as the final models within the noise (26.5-partial, above). **Limits:** random padding of up to 50 calls only; no white-box attack was run for the malware-only models; the effect is shown for these two model settings. Figure: `results/paper/B/figs/F14_padded_benign_false_alarms_final_settings` (new; SET B only; F13 keeps the earlier models of REPORT 5.10/5.11 in SET A).
