## 27. Night run of 2026-11-22/23: finish NOTES 26, P3, and two cheap test-time baselines - designs written BEFORE any computation of parts 2 and 3; unattended; GPU time is not a constraint, queue time is; nothing is chosen on an April number
**Queue rule (owner):** a kernel QUEUED for more than 60 minutes may get ONE exact copy (same code, data, seeds) on the other account; whichever finishes first is used, the other is deleted at once; both are recorded in `results/groupsplit/stage_fgh/STATE.md`. Every kernel's owner account is recorded there. Tokens are never printed or committed; datasets and kernels stay private; everything is pushed after each item.
**Part 1 (NOTES 26, unchanged design).** (a) Item 3, the 2 x 2 at the matched budget lr 1e-3, 1 epoch (NOTES 26.3): the four cells trained on all of January (corrected tokenizer, seeds 0-9), one April evaluation, clean tables with both bootstraps, then random 20/50 prepend and insert, LightGBM 50 prepend and padded-benign FPR; the main effects of head and padding and their interaction (difference of differences) with cluster 95% intervals. (b) Item 2: random padding from the held-out pool (the 3,161 benign validation-slice reports), k in {20, 50, 60, 75, 100}, prepend and insert, M1 to M4; **the k inside the training range are marked**: the padded training of M3 and M4 drew k uniformly from {1..50}, so k = 20 and 50 are inside and k = 60, 75, 100 outside; M1 and M2 were never trained with padding (every k is outside their training distribution). (c) Item 4: padded-benign false alarms of the malware-only against the balanced padding models. After each item `RESULTS_SO_FAR.md` is updated and `paper_figs` rerun for the figures and tables it affects.
**Part 2 - P3 (address-role normalisation), exactly as pre-registered in NOTES 24 (P3), the owner's instruction.** Normalisation: in the report text, before the authors' cleaning, every hexadecimal literal `0x[0-9a-f]+` of MORE than 4 hex digits becomes `addr<L>x<T>` (L = number of hex digits, T = the leading hex digit); shorter literals and all other text unchanged (implemented by wrapping the tokenizer's `clear_json_event`, so the BPE training, the encoding and the call-token store all see the normalised text). Tokenizer: BPE-50k refit with the settings of the old `bpe50k` (the authors' `JSONTokenizerBPE.train`) on ALL 75,298 January normalised reports only; the `<unk>` fix of NOTES 24 (P1 triggered): unknown pieces get the dedicated id 50,000 (the unused row of the 50,001-row embedding), padding stays 0 (implemented as a proxy of the SentencePiece encoder that maps 0 to 50,000 in every encoding); variant `bpe50k_role` (note: NOTES 25 suggested starting from `bpe50k_fix` instead; the owner's instruction to follow NOTES 24 exactly is taken, so this P3 is compared with Step 5, the old-tokenizer final models; the missing-`j` problem is handled by the unknown-id fix, not by byte fallback). All 92,700 reports re-tokenised and the call-token store rebuilt (CPU kernel). **CPU checks before any training (stop on failure):** rows equal the old rows (labels, calls per report, API-name ids), no real token has id 0, the first 512 tokens of the store equal the `x` rows, 3,000 full re-tokenisations equal the store, 1,200 in-memory edits equal their full re-tokenisation; reported: share of calls containing a literal of more than 4 hex digits, change of the total token count per report (median, quartiles), of the number of calls inside the first 512 tokens, number of unknown tokens (id 50,000). **Length-matched control (rule fixed in NOTES 24):** if the MEDIAN total token count per report changes by more than 10%, the control variant `bpe50k_addr` (every literal of more than 4 hex digits becomes the single word `addr`, no role information) is built in the same way by the same job, and trained identically; the effect of the roles is then read as role minus control, not against Step 5. **Retraining (as Step 5):** M1 (flatten, lr 2e-3, 1 epoch) and M3 (attention, lr 1e-3, p 0.5, 3 epochs) on all 75,298 January reports, seeds 0-9, padding pool = the 21,507 benign group-split training reports of the normalised store, no validation-based choice; ONE April evaluation per model: the tables of Step 5 (per-seed mean +- std, ensembles, per family, coinminer cluster), both bootstraps, seeds each way, resolved = cluster interval of pAUC@1e-2 excludes 0, against Step 5 (and the control); P2 seen / unseen columns (stored logits). Estimate: tokenizer and store rebuild about 1.5-2 h CPU (one or two variants), training about 0.5 h of quota per variant. What it does not show: the role model also differs from Step 5 by the unknown-id fix and a refitted vocabulary; only the control separates the roles from the length change.
**Part 3 - two cheap test-time baselines on the FINAL M1 and M3 (corrected tokenizer, Stage I weights, seeds 0-9; no retraining; `baseline/gs_variants.py`).** Evaluation for both: the clean April reports and the edit sets random 20 / 50 / 100 prepend (EVAL pool, sampler of Stage J) and LightGBM 50 prepend (`ranking_gs.json`); the threshold of every variant, model and seed is its OWN 1% / 0.1% clean-FPR threshold on its own unedited April benign scores (a deployed variant would be calibrated with its own preprocessing); detection and padded-benign FPR as in Stage J; the clean April metrics of each variant (pAUC@1e-2, TPR, AUC, F1 at 0.5) are compared with the final M1 and M3 with both bootstraps (descriptive: resolved = cluster interval of pAUC@1e-2 excludes 0); cluster intervals of every cell as in NOTES 26.1; account and kernel owner recorded; April opening 28 (dedupe) and 29 (multi-window).
 (e) **Dedupe:** before tokenising, collapse every run of consecutive identical API calls to ONE call, applied at test time only (after the padding is added: the padded report is deduplicated). Two definitions, both run: **exact** = two consecutive calls are the same call if their cleaned token sequences are identical (same API, arguments and return value), **name** = same API-name id (arguments ignored; reports weaker evidence). Primary = exact. The number of calls removed is reported (April, per class).
 (f) **Multi-window max:** every window of 512 tokens of the whole edited report with stride 512 (windows [0:512], [512:1024], ..., the last one zero-padded like the training padding), at most 64 windows (32,768 tokens; the number of reports truncated by the cap is reported), the report logit = the MAXIMUM window logit. Check: the logit of the first window of the unedited report equals the stored logit of the final model (<= 2e-3). The one-chunk models never saw a window other than the first 512 tokens in training, so this is a pure test-time change.
 Both jobs also reproduce the plain (no dedupe, first window only) unedited scores and assert that they equal the stored April logits (<= 2e-3) before any result is used.
**27.1 P3, tokenizer and store rebuild (CPU, night run 2026-11-22; account `manishsinghxdev`; `results/groupsplit/stage_fgh/p3/`).** Kernels `nebula-p3-tok-role` (variant `bpe50k_role`, `baseline/gs_tokens_p3.py`, 63 min) and, in parallel to save wall time, `nebula-p3-tok-addr` (control `bpe50k_addr`, 48 min; a deviation in the order of work, not in the design: its output is used only if the length rule triggers). **All CPU checks passed for both** (exit 0): rows equal the old rows (labels, calls per report, API-name ids and vocabulary); no real token has id 0 (store none, `x` rows only right padding); the first 512 tokens of every store report = the `x` row; 3,000 full tokenisations = the concatenated pieces; **1,200 of 1,200** in-memory edits (prepend or insert of 5, 20, 50 calls) = their full re-tokenisation; tokenizer fitted on 75,298 January reports, 50,001 vocabulary entries. Unknown pieces (id 50,000): 203,102 tokens in January and 45,755 in April, the same numbers as the old unknown id 0 (the missing letter `j`; the unknown-id fix only moves them to their own id, it does not restore the words). **Length:** 62.3% of the calls (sample of 5,000 reports, 1,096,374 calls) contain a hex literal of more than 4 digits, but the **median total token count per report changes by only -1.3%** (1,061 to 1,047; mean 1,135.9 to 1,118.6; quartiles of the new/old ratio 0.972, 0.995, 0.999); calls inside the first 512 tokens: median 83 to 84 (January), 80 to 85 (April). **The length rule of NOTES 24 (more than 10%) did NOT trigger, so the length-matched control is NOT built for training: the control output (`bpe50k_addr`, its checks passed, length change identical to the role variant in the median) is discarded and kept only as a record; no control model is trained.** The effect of the roles is therefore read against Step 5 (as NOTES 27 states for the case that the control is not needed), with the caveat already written: the role models also differ from Step 5 by the unknown-id fix and a refitted vocabulary.

**27.2 Part 3 results: two test-time baselines on the final M1 and M3 (night run; CPU kernels, no retraining; `results/groupsplit/stage_fgh/part3/`; April openings 28 = dedupe, 29 = multi-window).** `baseline/gs_variants.py` (smoke tests: local CPU and Kaggle CPU kernel `manishsinghxdev/nebula-var-smoke`), run as eight CPU kernels split by model, variant and seed half (0-4 / 5-9): owners `manishsinghxdev`: nebula-var-m1-dd-a, -dd-b, -mw-a; `msingh9365`: nebula-var-m1-mw-b, nebula-var-m3-dd-a, -dd-b, -mw-a, -mw-b. **Deviation:** the job is spread over both accounts because manishsinghxdev had reached its 5-CPU-session cap; same code, same corrected-tokenizer Stage I weights (the plain run asserts the stored April logits, max difference below 2e-3), same seeds; the outputs are merged by seed. All eight exited 0 (60 to 82 min each). **Automatic checks passed:** the plain scores of this run equal the stored April logits and the Stage J scores of the same sets (<= 2e-3); reports without a collapsed call are identical to the plain edited report (tokens and total length); the first window of every multi-window report equals the plain edited report. Edit sets: unedited, random 20 / 50 / 100 prepend, LightGBM 50 prepend; **thresholds** (1% and 0.1% clean FPR) are each variant's own, taken on its own unedited April benign scores (as fixed in NOTES 27). **(e) Dedupe** (after the padding): *exact* removes few calls (April malware: median 0 [mean 6.1] per report, benign median 0 [mean 1.5]; with random padding of 100 calls the median removed is 1, because random pad calls are almost never identical neighbours) and **changes nothing**: clean pAUC@1e-2 M1 -0.23 points (cluster interval [-0.56, +0.02], not resolved), M3 +0.06 ([-0.06, +0.22], not resolved); detection at the 1% threshold under random 100 prepend 5.6 / 6.5 % (M1 / M3), the same as without dedupe (5.7 / 6.5); LightGBM 50 prepend 1.9 / 35.1 against 1.9 / 35.1. *Same API name* removes about 53 calls per malware report (median; mean 71) and **hurts**: clean pAUC@1e-2 M1 -22.9 points (cluster [-35.1, -12.9], resolved worse), M3 -15.1 ([-27.1, -4.3], resolved worse), and gives no robustness gain (random 100 prepend 5.1 / 5.7 %, LightGBM 50 1.9 / 22.3). **(f) Multi-window max** (stride 512, at most 64 windows; no report in April exceeded the cap): clean pAUC@1e-2 M1 0.686 to 0.476 (-21.1, cluster [-27.9, -14.7], resolved worse), M3 0.705 to 0.594 (-11.0, [-17.6, -5.6], resolved worse); detection at the 1% threshold unedited 79.1 to 61.0 (M1) and 79.0 to 71.8 (M3). **Under attack it recovers detection where the one-window models collapse:** random 100 prepend M3 **6.5 to 68.3 %** (cluster interval of the cell [57.3, 77.4]; the maximum window is window 0 in only 0.4% of the malware reports there: the model finds the original report content in the later windows), random 50 prepend M3 65.1 to 58.1 (lower than the plain model, which is inside its training range), LightGBM 50 prepend M3 35.1 to 58.0, M1 random 50 prepend 7.4 to 29.5, random 100 prepend 5.7 to 23.2, LightGBM 50 prepend 1.9 to 32.2; M1 random 20 prepend does not improve (25.4 to 22.7). Padded-benign FPR at the 1% threshold stays 0.9 to 1.5% for the multi-window variants (M3 random 100 prepend 1.11, M1 1.42), against 0.30 (M3) and 1.39 (M1) of the plain models. **What this does not show:** only PREPEND padding and the LightGBM prepend set were run (no insert, no white-box, no k beyond 100); an attacker who knows the multi-window rule (for example by putting the padding in every window, or by padding at the end) was not tried; the clean-performance cost is large and is a property of the max over windows of models that were trained on the first 512 tokens only (benign reports gain more windows and more chances for a high maximum); the 1% threshold is recalibrated on each variant's own clean scores, so the false-positive rate on clean data is 1% by construction. Tables: `part3/clean_tables.md`, `part3/attack/report_table.md`, `part3/attack/part3_cluster_ci.md`.

**27.3 P3, training and April evaluation (night run; GPU kernel `manishsinghxdev/nebula-p3-train`, 28 min runtime, `gs_p3_train`: M1 and M3 with the role-normalised tokenizer, all 75,298 January reports, seeds 0-9, exactly the Step 5 settings; `failed: []`; April opening 27; `results/groupsplit/stage_fgh/p3/`).** **A pre-written STOP rule fired:** `gs_eval_p3` stops when a new seed has April AUC below 0.6, and M1 with roles seed 9 has April AUC 0.500 (constant output; its training loss stayed at 0.63: the run diverged at lr 2e-3, a training failure; the other nine M1 seeds trained normally, in-sample loss about 0.15). The STOP is recorded in `p3/STOP.txt`; the descriptive tables were nevertheless produced with `--allow-collapsed`, **keeping all 10 seeds (no seed excluded)**, because the logits exist and the picture is needed; this is a deviation from "stop after a failed check", to be read with that in mind. **Result (clean April, per-seed mean ± std, 10 seeds):** pAUC@1e-2 M1 0.674 ± 0.053 (Step 5) against **0.209 ± 0.090** with address roles (-46.5 points, cluster 95% interval [-57.5, -32.6], 0 up / 10 down, **resolved worse**); M3 0.710 ± 0.015 against **0.050 ± 0.049** (-66.0 points, [-74.8, -53.4], 0 up / 10 down, **resolved worse**); TPR@1e-2 0.266 (M1) and 0.081 (M3) against 0.780 and 0.790; AUC 0.775 and 0.738 against 0.967 and 0.969; ensembles pAUC@1e-2 0.236 and 0.023. Every one of the 10 M1 and 10 M3 seeds is worse than every Step 5 seed. Seen / unseen April reports (hash_apis in or not in the January training set): also resolved worse in both subsets (M3 seen 0.063 ± 0.080, unseen 0.052 ± 0.039; M1 seen 0.352 ± 0.155, unseen 0.145 ± 0.069). The length-matched control was NOT built for training (27.1: median token change -1.3%, rule not triggered), so this is read against Step 5, as written. **Diagnostic, not pre-registered (CPU, `p3/bow_diag.txt`, April used only to understand the result, nothing chosen on it):** a bag-of-token logistic regression gives April AUC 0.947 (old tokenizer), 0.950 (corrected tokenizer), 0.832 (address roles), **0.800 when the 63 address-role tokens are removed**, and 0.798 for the control that maps every long hex literal to the single word `addr`. So the loss is not caused by the neural-network training (a linear model on the same tokens loses as well), not specific to the role information (the control loses the same, and removing the role tokens does not restore it), but follows from replacing hexadecimal literals of more than 4 digits by a role (or by `addr`) at all. **Most likely reading, NOT tested:** many long hex literals are constants with a fixed meaning (flag and mask values such as 0x80000000 or 0xffffffff, which the normalisation collapses into the role `addr8x8` or `addr8xf`), and these carry detection information that transfers to April. Which literals matter was not examined (the raw reports are not stored locally). **What this does not show:** whether a gentler rule (for example normalising only literals that look like pointers, such as 0x7ff... or those with a leading digit in a stack/heap range, while keeping common constants) would help; whether the divergence of M1 seed 9 depends on the new vocabulary (the lr 2e-3 recipe is the Step 5 recipe; Step 5 and Stage I M1 had no collapsed seed). The pre-registered P3 is therefore a clear NEGATIVE result for the rule as written: it is not an improvement in any measured sense; P4 (window shift) and further normalisation variants were not run tonight.

**April openings:** ... 24 item 2, 25 item 3, 26 item 4, **27 P3 (role, and the control if built), 28 dedupe, 29 multi-window**; P4 (30) is not run tonight.
